//! Query result paths and nullable reads of private side tables.
use super::*;

pub(super) const VIEWER_KEY: &str = "$lookupViewerKey";
const TARGET_ROW: &str = "$lookupTargetRow";

// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#151-evidence-and-decision — compatibility keeps
// fallthrough null; check must name a concrete uncovered source path.
pub(super) fn fallthrough(decl: &ast::ProgramDecl) -> Diagnostic {
    fn path(body: &[S], implicit: bool) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
        let mut route = Vec::new();
        for (at, stmt) in body.iter().enumerate() {
            match stmt {
                S::Return { .. } => return None,
                S::Expr { .. } if implicit && at + 1 == body.len() => return None,
                S::Require { condition: A::Literal { value: Literal::Bool(false), .. }, .. } => return None,
                S::If { then_body, else_body, site, .. } => {
                    let completes = implicit && at + 1 == body.len();
                    let (arm, rest) = if let Some(rest) = path(then_body, completes) { ("true", rest) }
                        else if let Some(rest) = path(else_body, completes) { ("false", rest) }
                        else { return None };
                    route.push(format!("if at {site} is {arm}")); route.extend(rest);
                }
                S::For { site, .. } => route.push(format!("loop at {site} has zero iterations")),
                _ => {}
            }
        }
        Some(route)
    }
    let route = path(&decl.body, true).unwrap_or_default();
    let route = if route.is_empty() { "entry → end".into() } else { format!("{} → end", route.join(" → ")) };
    Diagnostic::new(&decl.site, "N_QUERY_FALLTHROUGH",
        format!("query {} can finish without a result ({route}); that path returns null", decl.name),
        "return a value on this path: add `return []` for an intentionally empty list, or `return null` for intentional absence; a return inside a possibly empty loop does not cover its zero-iteration path").guide("programs")
}

impl Binder<'_> {
    fn lookup_rule_proved(&self, value: &A) -> bool {
        let mut copy = value.clone();
        let mut target = false;
        super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |node| {
            if matches!(node, A::Name { name, .. } if name == TARGET_ROW) { target = true; }
        });
        if !target { return self.point_guard_safe(value) && self.authorization_proved(value, true); }
        match value {
            A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "or" => self.lookup_rule_proved(left) || self.lookup_rule_proved(right),
            A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" => self.lookup_rule_proved(left) && self.lookup_rule_proved(right),
            _ => false,
        }
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#152-lookup-contract-and-diagnostics — transport
    // only source advice facts across the helper's captured argument boundary.
    pub(super) fn helper_lookup_points(&self, function: &ast::FunctionDecl, args: &[A]) -> Vec<ReadExpr> {
        fn closed(value: &A, parameters: &[ast::ArgDecl]) -> bool {
            let mut value = value.clone();
            let mut safe = true;
            super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut value, &mut |node| match node {
                A::Name { name, .. } if name != "viewer" && name != "now" && !parameters.iter().any(|p| p.name == *name) => safe = false,
                A::Focal { .. } | A::Write { .. } | A::Call { .. } => safe = false,
                _ => {}
            });
            safe
        }
        fn substitute(value: &mut A, parameters: &[ast::ArgDecl], args: &[A]) {
            super::super::intent_walk::expression(value, &mut |node| {
                if let A::Name { name, .. } = node && let Some(at) = parameters.iter().position(|p| p.name == *name)
                    && let Some(argument) = args.get(at) { *node = argument.clone(); }
            });
        }
        fn collect(catalog: &Catalog, function: &ast::FunctionDecl, args: &[A], stack: &mut Vec<String>, fuel: &mut usize, reads: &mut Vec<ReadExpr>) {
            if stack.len() >= 32 || stack.contains(&function.name) { return; }
            stack.push(function.name.clone());
            let mut body = match &function.body { ast::FunctionBody::Block(body) => body.clone(), ast::FunctionBody::Expression(value) => vec![S::Return { value: value.clone(), site: value.site().clone() }] };
            super::super::intent_walk::statements(&mut body, &mut |node| {
                if *fuel == 0 { return; } *fuel -= 1;
                match node {
                    A::Read(read) if read.keys.iter().all(|key| closed(&key.value, &function.args)) => {
                        let mut value = A::Read(read.clone()); substitute(&mut value, &function.args, args);
                        if let A::Read(read) = value { reads.push(read); }
                    }
                    A::Call { name, args: arguments, .. } if arguments.iter().all(|value| closed(value, &function.args)) => {
                        if let Some(callee) = catalog.functions.get(name) {
                            let mut arguments = arguments.clone();
                            for value in &mut arguments { substitute(value, &function.args, args); }
                            collect(catalog, callee, &arguments, stack, fuel, reads);
                        }
                    }
                    _ => {}
                }
            });
            stack.pop();
        }
        if self.caller_kind != ProgramKind::Mutation { return Vec::new(); }
        let identity: Vec<_> = function.args.iter().map(|p| A::Name { name: p.name.clone(), site: p.site.clone() }).collect();
        let mut reads = Vec::new();
        collect(self.catalog, function, &identity, &mut Vec::new(), &mut 512, &mut reads);
        reads.into_iter().filter(|read| read.keys.iter().all(|key| closed(&key.value, &function.args))).filter(|read| {
            let mut value = A::Read(read.clone()); substitute(&mut value, &function.args, args);
            let A::Read(actual) = value else { return false };
            self.point_guard_safe(&A::Read(actual.clone())) && (self.point_present(&actual) || self.lookup_point_present(&actual) || self.row_independent_read_proved(&actual.table))
        }).collect()
    }
    // This marker suppresses advice only; it cannot authorize a runtime read
    // or enter the binder's authority/presence proof domains.
    pub(super) fn lookup_viewer_key(&self, value: &A) -> bool {
        if matches!(value, A::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer" || self.binding(name)
            .is_some_and(|binding| binding.fields.contains_key(&vec![VIEWER_KEY.into()]))) { return true; }
        let equal = A::Binary { op: "=".into(), left: Box::new(value.clone()), right: Box::new(A::Name { name: "viewer".into(), site: value.site().clone() }), site: value.site().clone() };
        self.point_guard_safe(value) && self.authorization_proved(&equal, true)
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#10-wave-20-loop-repairs-dx5 — asserted and same-table reads still need reader authority; blind writes do not pass here.
    pub(super) fn optional_lookup_note(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, point: &Read) {
        if self.caller_kind != ProgramKind::Mutation || self.point_present(read) || self.lookup_point_present(read)
            || self.row_independent_read_proved(&read.table) { return; }
        let table = &self.schema.tables[&read.table];
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() || self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table).is_some() { return; }
        let index = &table.indexes[&point.index];
        let pinned = index.components.iter().zip(&point.key).filter_map(|(part, value)| part.column().map(|name| (name, value))).collect();
        if snapback4_core::engine::rules::prefix_visibility(&read.table, table, &table.rules.read, &pinned).decided_true { return; }
        if let Some(rule) = self.catalog.tables[&read.table].decl.rules.iter().find(|rule| rule.effect == ast::Effect::Read) {
            let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
            let keys: BTreeMap<_, _> = read.keys.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(at, key)|
                Some((if named { key.column.clone()? } else { index.components.get(at)?.column()?.clone() },
                    if self.lookup_viewer_key(&key.value) { A::Name { name: "viewer".into(), site: key.site.clone() } } else { key.value.clone() }))).collect();
            let mut condition = rule.value.clone();
            // Rule constants and target fields occupy their own namespaces.
            // Freeze/seal them before injecting lexical caller key expressions.
            maintain_types::normalize_rule_constants(&mut condition);
            super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |value| {
                if let A::Member { base, .. } = value && let A::Focal { site } = base.as_ref() {
                    **base = A::Name { name: TARGET_ROW.into(), site: site.clone() };
                }
            });
            super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |value| {
                if let A::Member { base, name, .. } = value && matches!(base.as_ref(), A::Name { name, .. } if name == TARGET_ROW)
                    && let Some(key) = keys.get(name) { *value = key.clone(); }
            });
            if self.lookup_rule_proved(&condition) { return; }
        }
        let rule = table.through.as_ref().map_or_else(|| format!("read <- {}", table.rules.read.quarry()), |through| format!("read through .{}", through.reference));
        let location = self.catalog.tables[&read.table].decl.rules.iter().find(|rule| rule.effect == ast::Effect::Read)
            .map(|rule| rule.site.to_string()).unwrap_or_else(|| self.catalog.tables[&read.table].decl.site.to_string());
        if self.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_LOOKUP_FILTERED" && note.site == read.site) { return; }
        self.notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "N_LOOKUP_FILTERED",
            format!("mutation {}: lookup of {} uses `{rule}` ({location}); its key and preceding guards do not prove read authority. A hidden, expired or missing row returns null; `! else CODE` refuses with that authored code for all three. A later field/default or require may report a different business condition", self.name, read.table),
            "review whether these callers should read this data; use a deliberately readable projection containing only the needed fields, or keep null as intentional absence. An id and a later write rule do not grant read authority; do not widen private rows just to read one field").guide("rules"));
    }
}

fn period(schedule: &snapback4_core::schema::Schedule) -> String {
    schedule.describe()
}

/// What each schedule did on one clock advance, and why one did not fire.
/// The core fires each schedule at most once per advance, for the latest
/// boundary, and holds it while its saved boundary is ahead of the clock.
// @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — missed boundaries fire once; this only explains them.
pub fn schedule_report(engine: &Engine, before: &[Option<i64>], fired: &[String], now: i64, held: bool, each_boundary: bool, port: Option<u16>) -> Vec<serde_json::Value> {
    let after = saved_boundaries(engine);
    // Read only bounded summaries: job args/cursors and full error payloads
    // are irrelevant to this report and must not be loaded then replicated.
    let runs = job_summaries(engine).unwrap_or_default();
    engine.schema().schedules.iter().enumerate().map(|(index, schedule)| {
        let (old, saved) = (before.get(index).copied().flatten(), after.get(index).copied().flatten());
        let current = schedule.boundary(now);
        let next = schedule.next(current);
        let mut entry = serde_json::json!({ "name": schedule.name, "target": schedule.target, "period": period(schedule), "fired": false, "firedWindows": [], "skippedBoundaries": [], "nextBoundary": utc(next) });
        if fired.contains(&schedule.name) {
            let end = saved.unwrap_or(current);
            entry["fired"] = true.into();
            let start = schedule.previous(end);
            let window = |boundary| serde_json::json!({"periodStart": schedule.previous(boundary), "periodEnd": boundary});
            let mut windows = Vec::new();
            if each_boundary {
                if let Some(old) = old {
                    let mut at = schedule.next(old);
                    while at <= end { windows.push(window(at)); at = schedule.next(at); }
                }
            } else { windows.push(window(end)); }
            if each_boundary {
                entry["reason"] = format!("fired {} windows: {}", windows.len(), windows.iter().map(|window| format!("{} to {}", utc(window["periodStart"].as_i64().unwrap()), utc(window["periodEnd"].as_i64().unwrap()))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("; ")).into();
            }
            entry["firedWindows"] = windows.into();
            entry["periodStart"] = start.into();
            entry["periodEnd"] = end.into();
            if !each_boundary { entry["reason"] = format!("fired for the period {} to {}", utc(start), utc(end)).into(); }
            if let Some(run) = runs.get(&schedule.target) { entry["run"] = run.clone(); }
            // @ref LLP 3000.040#24-monthly-and-yearly-schedules — calendar periods count boundaries, not milliseconds.
            let skipped = if each_boundary { 0 } else { old.map(|old| schedule.periods_between(old, end) - 1).unwrap_or(0) };
            if skipped > 0 {
                entry["skippedPeriods"] = skipped.into();
                let mut windows = Vec::new();
                let mut at = schedule.next(old.unwrap());
                while at < end && windows.len() < SKIPPED_PREVIEW {
                    let mut item = window(at);
                    let mut args = serde_json::Map::new();
                    if let Some(program) = engine.program(&schedule.target) {
                        if program.args.contains_key("periodStart") { args.insert("periodStart".into(), schedule.previous(at).into()); }
                        if program.args.contains_key("periodEnd") { args.insert("periodEnd".into(), at.into()); }
                    }
                    item["command"] = format!("snapback4 run {} '{}'{}", schedule.target, serde_json::Value::Object(args), port.map(|port| format!(" --port {port}")).unwrap_or_default()).into();
                    windows.push(item); at = schedule.next(at);
                }
                entry["skippedBoundariesOmitted"] = (skipped - windows.len() as i64).into();
                entry["skippedBoundaries"] = windows.into();
                entry["reason"] = format!("fired once, for the latest period {} to {}; {skipped} earlier period{} this advance crossed (from {}) did not run: missed boundaries fire once. Run {} with periodStart/periodEnd for an earlier period",
                    utc(start), utc(end), if skipped == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, utc(old.unwrap_or(end)), schedule.target).into();
            }
        } else if held {
            entry["holdCode"] = "disabled".into();
            entry["reason"] = "schedules are held (dev --hold-schedules or --replay); a restart without the flag resumes at the next boundary".into();
        } else if let Some(saved) = saved.filter(|saved| old.is_some() && (*saved > current || *saved == current && *saved > schedule.boundary(snapback4_core::now_ms()))) {
            let due = schedule.next(saved);
            entry["holdCode"] = "saved-boundary".into();
            entry["savedBoundary"] = saved.into();
            entry["heldUntil"] = utc(due).into();
            entry["nextBoundary"] = utc(due).into();
            entry["reason"] = format!("held by the store: boundary {} was already processed and this clock {} has not reached its next window (restarting resets the process offset, not the saved boundary). One fire per boundary. Advance {} more, or use a fresh staging deployment/store for an independent test; keep the same --clock-offset when continuing a test across serve restarts",
                utc(saved), utc(now), span(due - now)).into();
        } else if old != saved {
            entry["holdCode"] = "initialized".into();
            entry["reason"] = format!("{} started at boundary {}: a schedule fires at the boundary after the first one it sees, next {} ({} from now)",
                if old.is_none() { "this schedule" } else { "this changed schedule" }, utc(saved.unwrap_or(current)), utc(next), span(next - now)).into();
        } else {
            entry["holdCode"] = "not-due".into();
            entry["reason"] = format!("next boundary {} ({} from now; schedule times are UTC)", utc(next), span(next - now)).into();
        }
        entry
    }).collect()
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#10-wave-20-loop-repairs-dx5 — every declared schedule has one reported outcome.
pub fn outcomes(schedules: &[serde_json::Value]) -> (Vec<serde_json::Value>, Vec<serde_json::Value>) {
    schedules.iter().cloned().partition(|schedule| schedule["fired"] == true)
}

/// The report never loads full error/argument payloads from retained jobs.
fn job_summaries(engine: &Engine) -> Result<std::collections::BTreeMap<String, serde_json::Value>, RuntimeError> {
    let mut summaries = std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
    for summary in engine.store().job_summaries(REPORT_ERROR_BYTES)? {
        let name = summary.name;
        if summaries.contains_key(&name) { continue; }
        let mut error = summary.error;
        let truncated = summary.error_truncated;
        if let Some(text) = &mut error && truncated {
            let mut end = REPORT_ERROR_BYTES.min(text.len());
            while !text.is_char_boundary(end) { end -= 1; }
            text.truncate(end); text.push('…');
        }
        summaries.insert(name, serde_json::json!({ "id":summary.id, "state":summary.state,
            "rowsWritten":summary.rows_written, "error":error, "errorTruncated":truncated }));
//! Advisory mismatches between a program's caller gate and its table rule.
use super::catalog::Catalog;
use crate::{ast::*, Diagnostic};
use snapback4_core::schema::{Schema, RuleExpr, RuleTerm};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
fn seat(expr: &Expr) -> Option<&str> {
    let Expr::Binary { op,left,right,.. }=expr else { return None; };
    if op == "and" { return seat(left).or_else(|| seat(right)); }
    if op != "=" { return None; }
    let name = |e: &Expr| matches!(e, Expr::Name { name,.. } if name == "viewer");
    let value = if name(left) { right.as_ref() } else if name(right) { left.as_ref() } else { return None; };
    match value { Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Str(s),.. } if s.starts_with("service:") => Some(s), _=>None }
}
fn fields(rule: &RuleExpr) -> Vec<String> {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer,RuleTerm::Field(f))|RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(f),RuleTerm::Viewer)=>vec![f.clone()],
        RuleExpr::And(rs)|RuleExpr::Or(rs)=>rs.iter().flat_map(fields).collect(),
        _=>vec![]
    }
}
fn required_owners(rule:&RuleExpr)->Vec<String> {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::And(parts)=>parts.iter().flat_map(required_owners).collect(),
        RuleExpr::Or(parts)=>{
            let Some(first)=parts.first() else {return vec![]};
            let mut common=required_owners(first);
            for part in &parts[1..] {let have=required_owners(part);common.retain(|f|have.contains(f));}
            common
        }
        other=>fields(other),
    }
}
fn admits_seat(rule: &RuleExpr, seat: &str) -> bool {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Allow|RuleExpr::Public { .. }=>true,
        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer,RuleTerm::Literal(v))|RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Literal(v),RuleTerm::Viewer)=>v.as_str().ok()==Some(seat),
        RuleExpr::Or(rs)=>rs.iter().any(|r|admits_seat(r,seat)),
        _=>false
    }
}
// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#7 — these notes state conditional argument requirements, never an impossibility theorem over dynamic inputs.
pub(super) fn notes(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
    let mut out=Vec::new();
    for doc in &catalog.documents {
        for table in &doc.tables {
            for column in &table.columns {
                if column.type_.kind==TypeKind::Principal && let Some(Expr::Literal {value:Literal::Str(value),..})=&column.default
                    && catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.personas).any(|p|p==value) {
                    out.push(Diagnostic::new(&column.site,"N_PERSONA_PRINCIPAL",format!("{}.{} defaults to bare persona '{value}', whose dev principal is 'dev:{value}'",table.name,column.name),format!("use `default 'dev:{value}'` for development, or supply the authenticated principal in the mutation for production")).guide("personas"));
                }
            }
        }
        for program in &doc.programs {
            if program.native { continue; }
            body(catalog,schema,program,&program.body,None,&mut out);
        }
    }
    out
}
fn body(catalog:&Catalog,schema:&Schema,program:&ProgramDecl,statements:&[Stmt], mut gate:Option<String>,out:&mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
    let mut lookups: BTreeMap<String,(String,Vec<String>)>=BTreeMap::new();
    for statement in statements {
        if let Stmt::Require { condition,code,site,.. }=statement {
            if program.kind==ProgramKind::Mutation && let Some(seat)=seat(condition) { gate=Some(seat.into()); }
            for (alias,(table,owners)) in &lookups {
                let mut uses_owner=false; let mut copy=condition.clone();
                super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy,&mut |e| if let Expr::Member { base,name,.. }=e && matches!(base.as_ref(),Expr::Name { name,.. } if name==alias) && owners.contains(name) { uses_owner=true; });
                if uses_owner && code != "NOT_FOUND" {
                    out.push(Diagnostic::new(site,"N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN",format!("{}: callers who cannot read {table} get NOT_FOUND at `{alias}`'s lookup, never {code} from this require",program.name),
                        format!("put a caller authorization check before the lookup if {code} must win; otherwise keep NOT_FOUND as the privacy-preserving answer and test it (guide rules)")).guide("rules"));
                }
            }
        }
        if let Stmt::Bind {names,..}=statement {for name in names {lookups.remove(name);}}
        if program.kind!=ProgramKind::Job && let Stmt::Bind { names,value:Expr::Assert { value,code,.. },.. }=statement && code=="NOT_FOUND" && let Expr::Read(read)=value.as_ref() {
            if let [name]=names.as_slice() && let Some(table)=schema.table(&read.table) {
                let owners=required_owners(&table.rules.read);
                if !owners.is_empty() { lookups.insert(name.clone(),(read.table.clone(),owners)); }
            }
        }
        let mut copy=vec![statement.clone()];
        // Inspect only this statement's expressions; nested control flow receives its own gate below.
        if !matches!(statement,Stmt::If { .. }|Stmt::For { .. }|Stmt::As { .. }) {
            super::intent_walk::statements(&mut copy,&mut |expr| {
                let Expr::Write { op,table,fields:payload,site,.. }=expr else { return; };
                let Some(def)=schema.table(table) else { return; };
                for field in payload.iter() {
                    let principal=catalog.tables.get(table).and_then(|t|t.columns.get(&field.name)).is_some_and(|t|t.kind==TypeKind::Principal);
                    if principal && let Some(Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Str(value),.. })=&field.value
                        && catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.personas).any(|p| p==value) {
                        out.push(Diagnostic::new(&field.site,"N_PERSONA_PRINCIPAL",format!("{table}.{} stores bare persona '{value}', but its dev viewer is 'dev:{value}'",field.name),format!("write 'dev:{value}' for development, or an authenticated account principal in production; personas are names, not principals")).guide("personas"));
                    }
                }
                if program.kind!=ProgramKind::Mutation { return; }
                // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#10-wave-20-loop-repairs-dx5 — source advice can name a private unique clause; public failures cannot.
                if matches!(op, WriteOp::Insert | WriteOp::Update | WriteOp::Upsert)
                    && (def.through.is_some() || def.not_before.is_some() || !matches!(def.rules.read, RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Public { .. }))
                    && !(def.through.is_none() && def.not_before.is_none() && gate.as_deref().is_some_and(|seat| admits_seat(&def.rules.read, seat)))
                    && let Some(refusals) = schema.unique_refusals.get(table) {
                    for (index_name, refusal) in refusals {
                        let Some(index) = def.index(index_name) else { continue; };
                        if *op == WriteOp::Update && !payload.iter().any(|field| field.spread || index.columns().contains(&field.name)) { continue; }
                        let location = catalog.tables.get(table).and_then(|t| t.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == index_name)).map(|i| &i.site).unwrap_or(site);
                        if out.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_UNIQUE_HIDDEN" && n.site == *location) { continue; }
                        let mut read = def.through.as_ref().map_or_else(|| format!("read <- {}", def.rules.read.quarry()), |through| format!("read through .{}", through.reference));
                        if let Some(column) = &def.not_before { read.push_str(&format!("; not before .{column}")); }
                        out.push(Diagnostic::new(location, "N_UNIQUE_HIDDEN",
                            format!("{table}'s unique {} declares else {}, but conflicts the caller cannot read under `{read}` use generic E_RULE (or an undisclosed target miss), never that named code. No stored conflict is inferred by this source note", index.columns().join(", "), refusal.code),
                            "keep private-row existence undisclosed; if the product requires a public availability check, model a minimal readable key projection or an operator-mediated claim. Test readable conflicts separately; do not widen private rows to obtain the named code").guide("rules"));
                    }
                }
                let Some(seat)=gate.as_deref() else { return; };
                let effect=match op { WriteOp::Insert=>"insert",WriteOp::Update=>"update", _=>return };
                let rule=if effect=="insert" { &def.rules.insert } else { &def.rules.update };
                if admits_seat(rule,seat) { return; }
                let others=required_owners(rule).into_iter().filter(|owner| payload.iter().find(|f|&f.name==owner).map_or(effect=="update",|f| {
                    let value=f.value.clone().unwrap_or_else(||Expr::Name { name:f.name.clone(),site:f.site.clone() });
                    !matches!(value,Expr::Name { ref name,.. } if name=="viewer") && !matches!(value,Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Str(ref s),.. } if s==seat)
                })).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                if others.is_empty() { return; }
                out.push(Diagnostic::new(site,"N_WRITE_DENIED",format!("table authorization layer: {} admits only '{seat}', but {table}'s {effect} rule `{}` requires the written {} to be this caller; writing another principal's value refuses E_RULE",program.name,rule.quarry(),others.join(", ")),
                    format!("if this trusted seat should write other principals' rows, amend `{effect} <- {}` to `{effect} <- ({}) or viewer = '{seat}'`; the mutation require alone grants no table authority (guide deploy, first admin); see: guide rule-recipes role-workflow",rule.quarry(),rule.quarry())).guide("rules"));
            });
        }
        match statement {
            Stmt::If { condition,then_body,else_body,.. }=> { body(catalog,schema,program,then_body,seat(condition).map(String::from).or_else(||gate.clone()),out); body(catalog,schema,program,else_body,gate.clone(),out); }
            Stmt::For { body:inner,.. }=>body(catalog,schema,program,inner,gate.clone(),out),
            Stmt::As { body:inner,.. }=>body(catalog,schema,program,inner,None,out),
            Stmt::Return { .. }|Stmt::Break { .. }|Stmt::Continue { .. }=>break,
            _=>{}
        }
    }
}

   const candidates = [join(dir, "override"), join(platformDir, "snapback4"), join(dir, "target/release/snapback4"), join(dir, "target/debug/snapback4")];
   for (const file of candidates) writeFileSync(file, "binary resolution fixture");
        match value {
            A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "or" => self.lookup_rule_proved(left) || self.lookup_rule_proved(right),
            A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" => self.lookup_rule_proved(left) && self.lookup_rule_proved(right),
            _ => false,
        }
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#152-lookup-contract-and-diagnostics — transport
    // only source advice facts across the helper's captured argument boundary.
    pub(super) fn helper_lookup_points(&self, function: &ast::FunctionDecl, args: &[A]) -> Vec<ReadExpr> {
        fn closed(value: &A, parameters: &[ast::ArgDecl]) -> bool {
            let mut value = value.clone();
            let mut safe = true;
            super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut value, &mut |node| match node {
                A::Name { name, .. } if name != "viewer" && name != "now" && !parameters.iter().any(|p| p.name == *name) => safe = false,
                A::Focal { .. } | A::Write { .. } | A::Call { .. } => safe = false,
                _ => {}
            });
            safe
        }
        fn substitute(value: &mut A, parameters: &[ast::ArgDecl], args: &[A]) {
            super::super::intent_walk::expression(value, &mut |node| {
                if let A::Name { name, .. } = node && let Some(at) = parameters.iter().position(|p| p.name == *name)
                    && let Some(argument) = args.get(at) { *node = argument.clone(); }
            });
        }
        fn collect(catalog: &Catalog, function: &ast::FunctionDecl, args: &[A], stack: &mut Vec<String>, fuel: &mut usize, reads: &mut Vec<ReadExpr>) {
            if stack.len() >= 32 || stack.contains(&function.name) { return; }
            stack.push(function.name.clone());
            let mut body = match &function.body { ast::FunctionBody::Block(body) => body.clone(), ast::FunctionBody::Expression(value) => vec![S::Return { value: value.clone(), site: value.site().clone() }] };
            super::super::intent_walk::statements(&mut body, &mut |node| {
                if *fuel == 0 { return; } *fuel -= 1;
                match node {
                    A::Read(read) if read.keys.iter().all(|key| closed(&key.value, &function.args)) => {
                        let mut value = A::Read(read.clone()); substitute(&mut value, &function.args, args);
                        if let A::Read(read) = value { reads.push(read); }
                    }
                    A::Call { name, args: arguments, .. } if arguments.iter().all(|value| closed(value, &function.args)) => {
                        if let Some(callee) = catalog.functions.get(name) {
                            let mut arguments = arguments.clone();
                            for value in &mut arguments { substitute(value, &function.args, args); }
                            collect(catalog, callee, &arguments, stack, fuel, reads);
                        }
                    }
                    _ => {}
                }
            });
            stack.pop();
        }
        if self.caller_kind != ProgramKind::Mutation { return Vec::new(); }
        let identity: Vec<_> = function.args.iter().map(|p| A::Name { name: p.name.clone(), site: p.site.clone() }).collect();
        let mut reads = Vec::new();
        collect(self.catalog, function, &identity, &mut Vec::new(), &mut 512, &mut reads);
        reads.into_iter().filter(|read| read.keys.iter().all(|key| closed(&key.value, &function.args))).filter(|read| {
            let mut value = A::Read(read.clone()); substitute(&mut value, &function.args, args);
            let A::Read(actual) = value else { return false };
            self.point_guard_safe(&A::Read(actual.clone())) && (self.point_present(&actual) || self.lookup_point_present(&actual) || self.row_independent_read_proved(&actual.table))
        }).collect()
    }
    // This marker suppresses advice only; it cannot authorize a runtime read
    // or enter the binder's authority/presence proof domains.
    pub(super) fn lookup_viewer_key(&self, value: &A) -> bool {
        if matches!(value, A::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer" || self.binding(name)
            .is_some_and(|binding| binding.fields.contains_key(&vec![VIEWER_KEY.into()]))) { return true; }
        let equal = A::Binary { op: "=".into(), left: Box::new(value.clone()), right: Box::new(A::Name { name: "viewer".into(), site: value.site().clone() }), site: value.site().clone() };
        self.point_guard_safe(value) && self.authorization_proved(&equal, true)
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#10-wave-20-loop-repairs-dx5 — asserted and same-table reads still need reader authority; blind writes do not pass here.
    pub(super) fn optional_lookup_note(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, point: &Read) {
        if self.caller_kind != ProgramKind::Mutation || self.point_present(read) || self.lookup_point_present(read)
            || self.row_independent_read_proved(&read.table) { return; }
        let table = &self.schema.tables[&read.table];
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() || self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table).is_some() { return; }
        let index = &table.indexes[&point.index];
        let pinned = index.components.iter().zip(&point.key).filter_map(|(part, value)| part.column().map(|name| (name, value))).collect();
        if snapback4_core::engine::rules::prefix_visibility(&read.table, table, &table.rules.read, &pinned).decided_true { return; }
        if let Some(rule) = self.catalog.tables[&read.table].decl.rules.iter().find(|rule| rule.effect == ast::Effect::Read) {
            let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
            let keys: BTreeMap<_, _> = read.keys.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(at, key)|
                Some((if named { key.column.clone()? } else { index.components.get(at)?.column()?.clone() },
                    if self.lookup_viewer_key(&key.value) { A::Name { name: "viewer".into(), site: key.site.clone() } } else { key.value.clone() }))).collect();
            let mut condition = rule.value.clone();
            // Rule constants and target fields occupy their own namespaces.
            // Freeze/seal them before injecting lexical caller key expressions.
            maintain_types::normalize_rule_constants(&mut condition);
            super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |value| {
                if let A::Member { base, .. } = value && let A::Focal { site } = base.as_ref() {
                    **base = A::Name { name: TARGET_ROW.into(), site: site.clone() };
                }
            });
            super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |value| {
                if let A::Member { base, name, .. } = value && matches!(base.as_ref(), A::Name { name, .. } if name == TARGET_ROW)
                    && let Some(key) = keys.get(name) { *value = key.clone(); }
            });
            if self.lookup_rule_proved(&condition) { return; }
        }
        let rule = table.through.as_ref().map_or_else(|| format!("read <- {}", table.rules.read.quarry()), |through| format!("read through .{}", through.reference));
        let location = self.catalog.tables[&read.table].decl.rules.iter().find(|rule| rule.effect == ast::Effect::Read)
            .map(|rule| rule.site.to_string()).unwrap_or_else(|| self.catalog.tables[&read.table].decl.site.to_string());
        if self.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_LOOKUP_FILTERED" && note.site == read.site) { return; }
        self.notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "N_LOOKUP_FILTERED",
            format!("mutation {}: lookup of {} uses `{rule}` ({location}); its key and preceding guards do not prove read authority. A hidden, expired or missing row returns null; `! else CODE` refuses with that authored code for all three. A later field/default or require may report a different business condition", self.name, read.table),
            "review whether these callers should read this data; use a deliberately readable projection containing only the needed fields, or keep null as intentional absence. An id and a later write rule do not grant read authority; do not widen private rows just to read one field").guide("rules"));
    }
}
of a readable, meter-admitted population. Preserve
non-uniform completeness and hidden-work cut redaction; do not grant row
access, invent rows, widen a rule, or label a partial empty result absence.
Regression witnesses compare branch and straight-line reads for each caller,
including self-referential rosters, indexed order, missing roles, and real
meter cuts. Any runtime defect exposed by these witnesses is fixed under
the existing authorization/meter contracts, not by relaxing them.

### 15.2 Lookup contract and diagnostics

Wave-20 advice amendment: LLP 3000.000.000.001 §10 extends N_LOOKUP_FILTERED
to asserted/same-table reads, preferring an existing N_LOOKUP_HIDDEN at the
same site; private unique source advice preserves the public refusal boundary.

Keep one point-read spelling. **`t[key]` returns the readable row or null**
for absent, hidden or expired rows. **`t[key] ! else CODE` refuses with CODE**
for the same three cases. This applies in queries and mutations and through
helpers. `exists t[key]` in a program is caller-visible; a rule's `exists`
is the separate rule-witness mechanism (§14.1). An id never grants authority.
There is no new optional-lookup operator or hidden-row error: the optional
form already exists. A later require cannot replace an earlier refusal.

Public refusal data must never distinguish hidden from missing, name a
runtime hiding clause only on a hit, disclose a key, or change a refusal's
meter according to existence. Static check diagnostics may quote authored
rules and source locations: they depend only on source, not stored rows.
Developer refusal traces remain local operator evidence. A blind write is
not a lookup and must not acquire a warning suggesting wider read access.

Extend program lookup advice beyond required same-table write targets.
**N_LOOKUP_FILTERED** identifies a mutation's optional point read of a
non-public side table (a table the operation does not write) when its key and
current binder flow facts do not prove read authority. Required direct point
reads keep existing N_LOOKUP_HIDDEN advice rather than duplicate this note;
the assertion already makes refusal explicit. It names
the operation, source point, target read rule and its declaration, and the
consequence (null before a later field/default or require).
Use the binder's actual point/key and current flow proofs, including helpers;
read-through side tables are included and name their authored inherited rule.
Ephemeral and maintained-value reads retain their specialized existing advice.
Captured helper arguments transport diagnostic-only exact-point facts (including
through nested helpers), plus known viewer identities. These markers cannot
establish runtime authority, non-null typing or prediction eligibility; key
rebinding and writes invalidate exact-point advice facts. The transfer walk is
bounded (512 visited expression nodes, depth 32); exhaustion declines the
suppression proof, never a runtime read or authority check;
never infer the read from a blind write target. Exact key pins that decide
read authority, a dominating readable lookup/guard, direct asserted reads and
lookups of the operation's written tables stay quiet. Rule constants are normalized before key substitution, and the target-row
namespace is sealed before injecting caller expressions: an unpinned target
field cannot borrow an enclosing shape's focal row or a caller binding.
This is missing-proof advice, not a claim all callers are denied. Repeated instances
are located, and advice suggests a deliberately reader-visible projection or
an earlier authorized gate; it never mechanically widens a private rule.
Existing N_LOOKUP_HIDDEN remains the more specific required write-target note;
avoid duplicate notes for that source site.

The rules card carries this lookup/refusal ladder and explicitly separates
program probes from rule probes. An executable packed-package qualification
runs every newly added/changed Quarry example as written and checks absent,
hidden, readable and expired rows plus statement precedence. Existing
SEC/SEC3 existence-oracle lints keep their source-level scope: a readable
row's custom later failure can intentionally reveal its existence, while
hidden and absent remain equivalent. SEC4 owns security-analysis changes.

### 15.3 Qualification and review
## 4. Staging clock

Wave-20 reporting amendment: LLP 3000.000.000.001 §10 changes the proof
advance return to `{now, fired, held}` and explains persisted boundary holds;
operator authority and clock execution semantics below are unchanged.

Only a store durably classified staging by deploy --staging may serve with
--clock-offset. Missing/unknown classification is production. This setting
is process-local, with the same signed-duration parser and monotone elapsed
clock as dev. No serve-time mechanism can reclassify an existing production store. Restart drops the offset unless explicitly
provided again; persisted timed-disclosure floors never retreat below their captured value.

Staging exposes the existing /dev/clock/status and /dev/clock/advance only
with any full service session: these are trusted operator credentials, as for
system jobs. Production refuses even with a service token, and an explicit
production serve offset refuses with E_CLOCK_DEV_ONLY before listener startup. Account, guest, read-only and unauthenticated
requests refuse. Recheck classification and session under the engine writer
before advancing. All fixture, reset, persona and dev-job doors remain closed.
Use the same advance implementation, schedule drain and ambiguous-reply law
as dev. CLI clock advance --release DIR mints its local service authority.
startServe accepts clockOffset and exposes now()/advance() through that
operator authority; it never changes store classification. Each helper control
mints a current staging-time operator session rather than caching a credential
across arbitrary time jumps. Ordinary user sessions retain normal expiry.

Hot release activation samples `shared.now()` under its engine writer, including
the persisted disclosure floor, and passes that one time through the core's
explicit `adopt_release_at`. A live preview probes the existing filesystem owner lock independently of
HTTP bind/address hints and acquires a stopped-store lock before taking its
SQLite snapshot, retaining that lock through inspection. A rollback preview
reuses its caller's existing ownership rather than probing its own lock as a
live host.
A live preview samples time through the same-owner,
store-bound existing private status protocol; no session is minted or clock
changed. A missing existing-store ownership sidecar refuses rather than assuming a
stopped host without exclusion; ordinary rollback preview can reestablish and
reuse its canonical ownership. A revision mismatch with the private database snapshot refuses as a
racing activation. Store contents and the clock are point-in-time samples, not
a freeze of later live writes. A stopped store's cold activation/preview uses
wall time, with the existing persisted disclosure floor applied by activation.
Positive and negative staging offsets are both covered by hot/preview tests.

        // JSON may escape every error byte; count that bound per schedule.
        report_bytes = report_bytes.saturating_add(2048 + 6 * (REPORT_ERROR_BYTES + 3) + 8 * (schedule.name.len() + schedule.target.len()));
        if report_bytes > REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET { return Err(crate::dev_clock::input("schedule report exceeds its byte budget; reduce the declared schedules")); }
        if held { continue; }
        let Some(old) = before.get(index).copied().flatten() else { continue; };
        let end = schedule.boundary(now);
        if old >= end { continue; }
        let periods = schedule.periods_between(old, end);
        if each_boundary && periods > (10_000 - boundaries.len()) as i64 {
            return Err(crate::dev_clock::input("--each-boundary crosses more than 10000 schedule windows; use a smaller duration or omit --each-boundary to coalesce"));
        }
        report_bytes = report_bytes.saturating_add((if each_boundary { periods as usize } else { (periods as usize).min(SKIPPED_PREVIEW + 1) }).saturating_mul(1024 + 8 * schedule.target.len()));
        if report_bytes > REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET { return Err(crate::dev_clock::input("schedule window report exceeds its byte budget; use a smaller duration")); }
        if !each_boundary { continue; }
        let mut boundary = schedule.next(old);
        while boundary <= end {
            boundaries.push((index, boundary));
            boundary = schedule.next(boundary);
        }
    }
    boundaries.sort_by_key(|(index, at)| (*at, *index));
    Ok(boundaries)
}

fn period(schedule: &snapback4_core::schema::Schedule) -> String {
    schedule.describe()
}

/// What each schedule did on one clock advance, and why one did not fire.
/// The core fires each schedule at most once per advance, for the latest
/// boundary, and holds it while its saved boundary is ahead of the clock.
// @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — missed boundaries fire once; this only explains them.
pub fn schedule_report(engine: &Engine, before: &[Option<i64>], fired: &[String], now: i64, held: bool, each_boundary: bool, port: Option<u16>) -> Vec<serde_json::Value> {
    let after = saved_boundaries(engine);
    // Read only bounded summaries: job args/cursors and full error payloads
    // are irrelevant to this report and must not be loaded then replicated.
    let runs = job_summaries(engine).unwrap_or_default();
    engine.schema().schedules.iter().enumerate().map(|(index, schedule)| {
        let (old, saved) = (before.get(index).copied().flatten(), after.get(index).copied().flatten());
        let current = schedule.boundary(now);
        let next = schedule.next(current);
        let mut entry = serde_json::json!({ "name": schedule.name, "target": schedule.target, "period": period(schedule), "fired": false, "firedWindows": [], "skippedBoundaries": [], "nextBoundary": utc(next) });
        if fired.contains(&schedule.name) {
            let end = saved.unwrap_or(current);
            entry["fired"] = true.into();
            let start = schedule.previous(end);
            let window = |boundary| serde_json::json!({"periodStart": schedule.previous(boundary), "periodEnd": boundary});
            let mut windows = Vec::new();
            if each_boundary {
                if let Some(old) = old {
                    let mut at = schedule.next(old);
                    while at <= end { windows.push(window(at)); at = schedule.next(at); }
                }
            } else { windows.push(window(end)); }
            if each_boundary {
                entry["reason"] = format!("fired {} windows: {}", windows.len(), windows.iter().map(|window| format!("{} to {}", utc(window["periodStart"].as_i64().unwrap()), utc(window["periodEnd"].as_i64().unwrap()))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("; ")).into();
            }
            entry["firedWindows"] = windows.into();
            entry["periodStart"] = start.into();
            entry["periodEnd"] = end.into();
            if !each_boundary { entry["reason"] = format!("fired for the period {} to {}", utc(start), utc(end)).into(); }
            if let Some(run) = runs.get(&schedule.target) { entry["run"] = run.clone(); }
            // @ref LLP 3000.040#24-monthly-and-yearly-schedules — calendar periods count boundaries, not milliseconds.
            let skipped = if each_boundary { 0 } else { old.map(|old| schedule.periods_between(old, end) - 1).unwrap_or(0) };
            if skipped > 0 {
                entry["skippedPeriods"] = skipped.into();
                let mut windows = Vec::new();
                let mut at = schedule.next(old.unwrap());
                while at < end && windows.len() < SKIPPED_PREVIEW {
                    let mut item = window(at);
                    let mut args = serde_json::Map::new();
                    if let Some(program) = engine.program(&schedule.target) {
                        if program.args.contains_key("periodStart") { args.insert("periodStart".into(), schedule.previous(at).into()); }
                        if program.args.contains_key("periodEnd") { args.insert("periodEnd".into(), at.into()); }
                    }
                    item["command"] = format!("snapback4 run {} '{}'{}", schedule.target, serde_json::Value::Object(args), port.map(|port| format!(" --port {port}")).unwrap_or_default()).into();
                    windows.push(item); at = schedule.next(at);
                }
                entry["skippedBoundariesOmitted"] = (skipped - windows.len() as i64).into();
                entry["skippedBoundaries"] = windows.into();
                entry["reason"] = format!("fired once, for the latest period {} to {}; {skipped} earlier period{} this advance crossed (from {}) did not run: missed boundaries fire once. Run {} with periodStart/periodEnd for an earlier period",
                    utc(start), utc(end), if skipped == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, utc(old.unwrap_or(end)), schedule.target).into();
            }
        } else if held {
            entry["holdCode"] = "disabled".into();
            entry["reason"] = "schedules are held (dev --hold-schedules or --replay); a restart without the flag resumes at the next boundary".into();
        } else if let Some(saved) = saved.filter(|saved| old.is_some() && (*saved > current || *saved == current && *saved > schedule.boundary(snapback4_core::now_ms()))) {
            let due = schedule.next(saved);
            entry["holdCode"] = "saved-boundary".into();
            entry["savedBoundary"] = saved.into();
            entry["heldUntil"] = utc(due).into();
            entry["nextBoundary"] = utc(due).into();
            entry["reason"] = format!("held by the store: boundary {} was already processed and this clock {} has not reached its next window (restarting resets the process offset, not the saved boundary). One fire per boundary. Advance {} more, or use a fresh staging deployment/store for an independent test; keep the same --clock-offset when continuing a test across serve restarts",
                utc(saved), utc(now), span(due - now)).into();
        } else if old != saved {
            entry["holdCode"] = "initialized".into();
            entry["reason"] = format!("{} started at boundary {}: a schedule fires at the boundary after the first one it sees, next {} ({} from now)",
                if old.is_none() { "this schedule" } else { "this changed schedule" }, utc(saved.unwrap_or(current)), utc(next), span(next - now)).into();
        } else {
            entry["holdCode"] = "not-due".into();
            entry["reason"] = format!("next boundary {} ({} from now; schedule times are UTC)", utc(next), span(next - now)).into();
        }
        entry
    }).collect()
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#10-wave-20-loop-repairs-dx5 — every declared schedule has one reported outcome.
pub fn outcomes(schedules: &[serde_json::Value]) -> (Vec<serde_json::Value>, Vec<serde_json::Value>) {
    schedules.iter().cloned().partition(|schedule| schedule["fired"] == true)
}

/// The report never loads full error/argument payloads from retained jobs.
fn job_summaries(engine: &Engine) -> Result<std::collections::BTreeMap<String, serde_json::Value>, RuntimeError> {
    let mut summaries = std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
    for summary in engine.store().job_summaries(REPORT_ERROR_BYTES)? {
        let name = summary.name;
        if summaries.contains_key(&name) { continue; }
        let mut error = summary.error;
        let truncated = summary.error_truncated;
        if let Some(text) = &mut error && truncated {
            let mut end = REPORT_ERROR_BYTES.min(text.len());

// Reset even when a debug/unwind request panics mid-drain. Engine poison
// recovery and durable work cursors retain the normal retry semantics.
struct Advancing<'a>(&'a Shared);
impl Drop for Advancing<'_> {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        let mut state = lock(&self.0.maintenance);
        state.advancing = false;
        state.next = BulkKind::Delivery;
    }
}

fn completed_drain(work: Work, stopping: bool) -> Result<Work, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    if stopping {
        return Err(snapback4_core::Refusal::new("E_CLOCK_INTERRUPTED", "clock", "clock advanced, but host shutdown interrupted the drain; inspect queued work instead of repeating the advance").guide("clock"));
    }
    Ok(work)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#4-staging-clock — staging grants clock control, never fixture or persona authority.
pub(super) fn staging(shared: &Shared) -> bool {
    lock(&shared.engine).store().meta("deployment_environment").ok().flatten().as_deref() == Some("staging")
}
fn clock_service(request: &Request, shared: &Shared) -> Result<Option<String>, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    if shared.personas { return Ok(None); }
    if !staging(shared) { return Err(crate::dev_clock::release()); }
    match super::session_identity::principal_of(request,shared) {
        Ok((principal,kind,snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full)) if kind == "service" && principal.starts_with("service:") => Ok(Some(principal)),
        _ => Err(snapback4_core::Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "staging clock control requires a full service token").guide("clock")),
    }
}
pub(super) fn advance(request: Request, shared: &Shared, body: &str) {
    let service = match clock_service(&request, shared) {
        Ok(service) => service,
        Err(why) => return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&why)})),
    };
    let payload = match serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(body) {
        Ok(payload) => payload,
        Err(_) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::input("clock advance takes {\"duration\":\"+2m\"}"))})),
    };
    let each_boundary = match payload.get("eachBoundary") {
        None => false,
        Some(serde_json::Value::Bool(value)) => *value,
        Some(_) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::input("eachBoundary must be a boolean"))})),
    };
    let duration = payload.get("duration").and_then(|value| value.as_str());
    let milliseconds = match duration.ok_or_else(|| crate::dev_clock::input("clock advance takes {\"duration\":\"+2m\"}"))
        .and_then(crate::dev_clock::duration) {
        Ok(milliseconds) => milliseconds,
        Err(error) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
    };
    // Serialize clock callers only. Background slices check advancing after
    // engine admission and skip during this synchronous drain.
    let advance = lock(&shared.maintenance).advance.clone();
    let _advance = lock(&advance);
    let _advancing = Advancing(shared);
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12 — simulated time makes retries due without stealing a live worker's claim or viewer session.
    let advanced = (|| {
        // Match claims' engine-then-clock lock order: no claim can land
        // between shifting its lease and publishing the advanced clock.
        let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
        let before = crate::dev_clock_store::saved_boundaries(&engine);
        if let Some(principal) = &service {
            if engine.store().meta("deployment_environment").map_err(|e|e.refusal())?.as_deref() != Some("staging") { return Err(crate::dev_clock::release()); }
            super::session_identity::mutation_authority(&request,shared,&engine,principal)?;
        } else { shared.dev_authority(&engine)?; }
        let mut clock = lock(&shared.clock);
        let mut next = clock.clone();
        let now = next.advance(milliseconds)?.max(shared.time_audience_clock.load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Acquire));
        let boundaries = crate::dev_clock_store::crossed_boundaries(&engine, &before, now, shared.hold_schedules, each_boundary)?;
        lock(&shared.maintenance).advancing = true;
        engine.advance_worker_leases_at(milliseconds, clock.now()).map_err(|e| e.refusal())?;
        *clock = next;
        let offset = clock.offset();
        let skew = now - snapback4_core::now_ms();
        let isolated = if crate::dev_clock_store::has_data(&engine) {
            format!("; use {} for isolated time tests; clock advance --port N targets its printed port (guide jobs)", crate::dev_clock::isolated_command())
        } else { String::new() };
        let message = format!("cumulative offset {offset:+}ms (requested process offset; now includes the authoritative floor); process offset lasts until restart{isolated}");
        Ok((now, offset, skew, message, before, boundaries))
    })();
    let (now, offset, skew, message, before, boundaries) = match advanced {
        Ok(result) => result,
        Err(error) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
    };
    // @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — a developer is awaiting this
    // request; synchronous clock-advance work intentionally keeps request QoS.
    let drained = (|| {
        let mut crossed = Vec::new();
        if each_boundary {
            for (index, at) in boundaries {
                crossed.extend(engine_work(shared, |engine, _| engine.fire_schedule_boundary_at(shared.now(), index, at))?);
            }
        }
        let mut work = work_at(shared, true, 0, Some(now))?;
        work.schedules += crossed.len();
        work.fired.extend(crossed);
        Ok::<_, RuntimeError>(work)
    })();
    match drained.map_err(|error| error.refusal()).and_then(|work| completed_drain(work, crate::lifecycle::stopping())) {
        Ok(work) => {
            let schedules = crate::dev_clock_store::schedule_report(&lock(&shared.engine), &before, &work.fired, now, shared.hold_schedules, each_boundary, shared.dev_identity.as_ref().map(|i| i.address.port()));
            let (fired, held) = crate::dev_clock_store::outcomes(&schedules);
            respond(request, 200, json!({"now":now, "advancedMs":milliseconds, "sessionNotice":crate::dev_clock::SESSION_NOTICE, "offsetMs":offset, "skewMs":skew, "eachBoundary":each_boundary, "message":message, "work":work, "schedules":schedules, "fired":fired, "held":held}))
        }
        // Time remains advanced if maintenance fails; callers must not
        // retry the advance blindly. The normal tick retries pending work.
        Err(error) => {
            let engine = lock(&shared.engine);
            let after = crate::dev_clock_store::saved_boundaries(&engine);
            let fired: Vec<_> = engine.schema().schedules.iter().enumerate().filter(|(i, _)| {
                matches!((before.get(*i).copied().flatten(), after.get(*i).copied().flatten()), (Some(old), Some(new)) if new > old)
            }).map(|(_, schedule)| schedule.name.clone()).collect();
            let mut schedules = crate::dev_clock_store::schedule_report(&engine, &before, &fired, now, shared.hold_schedules, each_boundary, shared.dev_identity.as_ref().map(|i| i.address.port()));
            for (index, schedule) in schedules.iter_mut().enumerate() {
                if schedule["fired"] != true && !shared.hold_schedules && before.get(index).copied().flatten().is_some_and(|old| old < engine.schema().schedules[index].boundary(now)) {
# DX5 — wave 20, target 0.4.36

## Governing context

Used llp-orient. Snapback 4 fence/rules, LLP 3000 §7, LLP 2000.000,
3000.000.000.001 §§8–10, 3000.000.000.002 §4, 3000.036.002 §15.2,
3000.013.002 §2, and 3000.011.003.000 §§7.2/11 govern this work.
The full wave-20 bakeoff was read; its clock035 repro supplies the reused-store
case. SEC5 retains security intent/campaign/exposure ownership. DX5's amendment
is §10 of LLP 3000.000.000.001; pointers update the other governing LLPs.

## Changes and commits

1. **Staging clock:** structured `{now, fired, held}`, bounded schedule/run
   explanations, equal/future persisted-boundary stderr notices, incomplete
   failure reports and no automatic retry. Expiry notice no longer discusses
   dev doors. Timestamp users read `.now`. Commit `1446521e03` plus final
   follow-up pending. Tests use the real staged HTTP host, restart the same
   store, show a due pending row held, and recover at the next boundary.
2. **Lookup/conflict diagnostics:** asserted and same-table mutation reads get
   source advice naming the read rule. Existing specific N_LOOKUP_HIDDEN takes
   precedence. Named private unique declarations explain why hidden conflicts
   keep E_RULE; readable conflicts keep the declared code. No runtime authority
   or hidden-row disclosure change. Paired wire/receipt and equal-input-size
   meter controls. Commit `addd95471a`.
3. **Upgrade:** bounded multiline shell/argv candidate scan and first-check
   warning delivery in the newly installed wrapper. Captured stderr cannot
   acknowledge nonempty warnings, so old quiet upgrades leave advice pending.
   All 17 known wave-19 production-script lanes are found, with no extras in
   that census. One-line migration: `JSON.parse(stdout).rows`.
4. **Frontend/starter:** ReadState, packaged Expo Metro transformer and native
   smoke hook, Playwright in every Expo starter, retained runnable frontend
   proof scaffold that explicitly refuses missing business assertions. Adapt
   preserves old README recognition and custom helpers/proofs; removes an
   untouched helper only after all planned local references disappear.

Design/review checkpoint: `e8acda1db8`. Initial Muse READY; initial fresh Codex
found swallowed old quiet warnings. Round 2 found the remaining timestamp
consumer and old Metro README/test custody; those findings have been repaired.
Round 3 readiness is pending. Verbatim transcripts and provenance are under
`llp/reviews/dx5/`.
