## 2. A short common read

Wave-20 list presentation addition: LLP 3000.000.000.001 §10 defines the
packaged ReadState component; the query hooks below retain their contracts.

Add `useQueryData(op, args, options?)` in `snapback4/react`, shared by React
and Expo. It delegates directly to `useQuery` and returns `R | undefined`:
loading gives undefined; present, absent and partial give their exact data
(including null, empty lists, false and zero). A denied read throws
`QueryRefusal extends Error`, carrying the original typed `why: Refusal`.
An error boundary may render it; callers choosing inline refusal UI use
`useQueryResult`, which returns `{data: R | undefined, why: Refusal | null,
read: Read<R>}` without throwing. These hooks do not substitute an empty list for loading/refusal. They add
no cache, previous-argument retention or observation ownership. `read` is the same snapshot as `useQuery`.

A data-only read does not promise completeness or freshness. It is for
screens that show the authorized rows that arrived. Pagination, absence proofs,
and actions depending on fresh or complete results continue to use `useQuery`
or `usePage` and their metadata. The precise API remains unchanged. Skipping
uses the existing typed arguments and `{skip: true}`. Required/mistyped
arguments still fail typechecking; result types come from generated operations.
`useMutation(op).run` is already a typed callable and remains unchanged.

Add `<SignedIn fallback={...} restoring={...}>...</SignedIn>` inside
AppProvider (bare SnapbackProvider is insufficient), using the same
`useAuth()` snapshot as AppProvider. Signed-in children mount only in that
state; restoring defaults to null, signed-out uses fallback. The existing
AppProvider account boundary resets account-owned state. Children must be JSX
components (React elements), not eagerly invoked component functions.
No bundled login UI or new session runtime is added to the React entry.

## 3. Completeness notes

Default check output leads with a checked repair against the author's own
### 7.2 Wave 3 (0.4.18): init first, schema second

Wave-20 custody amendment: LLP 3000.000.000.001 §10 keeps a runnable frontend
proof scaffold across adapt and moves Expo Metro/native smoke support into
the adapter package; edited application files remain owned by the app.

Wave 3 of the app farm (100 agents on 0.4.18) showed that §7.1's fixes fire
only when the schema exists *before* `init`. The README and the brief lead
agents to `init --web/--expo` first; they then replace `snapback/schema.q` and
are left with the todo starter's `fixtures/todos.jsonl` and cases (`test` runs
nothing), a guest sign-in screen over a persona-only schema (`auth.guest()`
refuses `E_AUTH`), or, rerunning init to get the picker, `E_INIT_CONFLICT`
(21 lanes, 41 minutes). The decisions, which make the outcome independent of
the order:

- **Leftovers are judged by the starter's bytes.** A starter file is a
  leftover only while it is byte-identical to what init wrote (the screen
  and client compared after normalizing the backend port and the persona
  list, the two values init varies) *and* it no longer fits the schema: the
  fixtures and cases when no `todos` table with `list`/`add`/`toggle`/`remove`
  exists; the screen and client when the schema's sign-in wants the other
  screen (guest for `use identity` with guests, the persona picker for
  `use personas` without `use identity`, or a picker for a different cast).
  A file the project changed is never reported as a leftover and never
  touched. No manifest is written: the starter text embedded in the binary is
  the reference, so a project that has never run 0.4.19's init still qualifies.
- **`check` names them, `init --adapt` fits them.** A successful `check`
  ends with a `starter:` block listing each leftover and its reason, then
  one command, `snapback4 init --adapt` (`check --json`: `starterLeftovers`
  with `files`, `advice` and `fix`). It never changes the exit status.
  `init --adapt [dir]` removes the unmodified fixtures (0.4.19 also removed
  an emptied `fixtures/`; §7.4 reverses that), and rewrites the unmodified screen and client to exactly
  what schema-first init writes for the same schema and port. A password-only
  or identity-less schema, and an adapted screen whose `Todos` component still
  calls the starter's operations, get advice lines rather than a rewrite.
  `--adapt` accepts only `--web`/`--expo` beside it and refuses without a
  `snapback/*.q`.
- **A project's `snapback/*.q` is never a conflict.** With any `.q` present,
  init leaves the starter's `schema.q` out with or without `--skip-existing`,
  lists the project's `.q` files as kept, and never writes through a linked
  schema. This replaces §7.1's "a conflict (or, with `--skip-existing`, kept
  out)". When an `E_INIT_CONFLICT` lists the files of an earlier starter that
  `--adapt` would fit, its first line and its rewrite name `init --adapt`.
- `test`'s refusal of a stray `todos.jsonl` names `init --adapt` as well.

Not done: `test` does not silently skip starter fixtures; a run that
executes nothing stays a refusal, and the command to fix it is one line.
(§7.4 skips the unmodified starter rows loudly instead.)

# Client and operations

[Back to the package guide](./README.md).

For TypeScript actions use the generated `server()` wrapper described in
[server handlers](./SERVER.md); it enforces the application's precise handler
signatures. A custom runtime host can use the lower-level registry entry below
with that generated manifest and already checked handlers. It does not infer
application-specific argument/result types itself:

```ts
import { startServer, type ServerHandlers } from "snapback4/server";
import { manifest } from "../snapback/generated/server";

function hostCheckedHandlers(handlers: ServerHandlers, signal: AbortSignal) {
  return startServer(manifest, handlers, { signal });
}
```

## Offline web starter

`npx snapback4 init --web` writes a one-screen Vite + React todos app on
`createApp` with `webDevice()`; `npx snapback4 init --web --template chat`
copies the maintained Vite chat reference, including its production app shell,
account-bound saved route and write IDs. Default `init` remains schema-only.
Run `npm install`, `npx snapback4 check`, then
`npx snapback4 dev` and `npm run dev` in separate terminals. Build with
`VITE_SNAPBACK_URL=https://your-backend npm run build`; serve `dist/` over HTTPS.
The chat's routes use `#room=<id>`.

The build ends with `snapback4 web-shell dist`. Navigations use the active
worker's cached HTML even online, so its HTML and lazy assets stay together
until a waiting build activates after the old tabs close. This command also works on
`expo export -p web` output: it precaches every file in a content-hashed cache,
registers `snapback4-sw.js` in every HTML file idempotently, and resolves exported
subpages (`/contact` and `/contact/` to `contact.html`, then `/about/` to
`about/index.html` when `about.html` is absent) online and offline before falling
back to the root `index.html`. Pass `--base /chat/` when serving a subpath;
use the same base in the bundler. For Vite run
`npx vite build --base=/chat/ && npx snapback4 web-shell dist --base /chat/`.
It waits for old tabs to close before switching builds. Development has no worker.
Keep all exported files nonpersonalized; this is a static export, not SSR.
Cross-origin requests and non-exported API/media requests pass through untouched.
The worker never caches network navigation responses. The starter explicitly uses
`localStorageSessionStore()` for page-restart continuity; any script on that
origin can read the token. Choose this persistence deliberately.

Held room navigation reads conversation and membership before rendering the
thread; unknown offline rooms remain uncertain. Write references are saved
before SDK dispatch and reopened with `usePendingWrite(op, id)`, without
resubmission. `markRead` demonstrates a nonpredicting queued write. Failed and
unknown receipts stay visible; a settled write does not imply a fresh read.
Sign-out hides the account, completes only after local credentials are removed,
and keeps its replica/outbox intact. A storage failure stays visible for retry.
For a keyed text/media send, use the intent-card recipe below; keep saved failure references scoped to this account and backend.
See the generated README for residency tables, horizon choices and the
production offline-reopen journey; fixture residency remains
`test --residency --fixture <directory> --as <principal>`.

For a browser proof, register the application's own app and generated API
(starters do this; the hook is active only under `?witness=web`):

```ts
import { registerWebWitness, witnessForm } from "snapback4/witness-web";
import { createApp } from "snapback4/app";
import { localStorageSessionStore } from "snapback4/auth";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
const app = createApp({ url: "http://127.0.0.1:4400", sessionStore: localStorageSessionStore() });
registerWebWitness(app, api);
export function submitMessage(event: Event, conversationId: string, body: string) {
  return witnessForm(app, event, async mutate => { await mutate(api.send, { conversationId, body }); });
}
```

`npm run build:web && snapback4 witness web dist` proves the first durable
mutation reaches `sent`; `--mutation NAME --args JSON` supplies application
values. Password accounts use `--email EMAIL --password-env ENV_VAR`, with
`--url URL` for an existing backend. See `guide testing` for lifecycle and
idempotence limits; add `--field Task --value "Browser proof" --button Add` for the starter's visible form. The observer arms before clicking and requires that write's sent receipt.

## 4. The client

**Constructor compatibility:** ready `Read<T>` values now require `existence`. Readers keep `isReady`/`isLoading`/`isDenied`; custom clients and mocks must classify their published value as `present`, `absent` or `partial`. No wire field was added. See `guide client` for the rules and point-read narrowing.

`createApp` is the ordinary entry point. Construct it once outside React render.

| Program | Constructor |
|---|---|
| Node script, seed, test or worker | `createClient({ url, persona })` from `snapback4/client` in development; `createClient({ url, session })` after `signup`/`login` from `snapback4/auth` |
| Browser app, no synced table | `createApp({ url, sessionStore: localStorageSessionStore() })` under `AppProvider`: writes are journaled without a device, no wasm (below) |
| Browser app with synced tables | `createApp({ url, device: webDevice(), sessionStore })` under `AppProvider`; the device is about 650 KB gzip of wasm (below) |
| Expo app | `createApp({ url, device: nativeAvailable() ? nativeDevice : undefined, sessionStore })`; `webDevice()` on web |

**Browser writes need `createApp`.** A bare `createClient()` in a browser has
no synchronous write journal and refuses every mutation with `E_ADMISSION`;
reads still work. `createApp` journals each click before `mutate` returns: a
write survives a reload or a same-turn navigation, and the server runs it at
most once. When no table declares `sync`, it does this without a device (see
"Browser writes without a device"); synced tables need `webDevice()`.

# Staging with real users

Staging runs real password users and application rules under serve. Use it for shared previews and probe:serve, with roles provisioned by a local operator.

```quarry
use identity password
table roles:
  principal: principal
  role: enum('admin', 'member')
  unique principal
  immutable principal
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:operator'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:operator'
  update <- viewer = 'service:operator'
  online only
mutation provision(principal: principal, role: enum('admin', 'member')):
  require viewer = 'service:operator' else NOT_OPERATOR
  return upsert roles[principal] { principal, role }
query myRole():
  return roles[viewer]
table notes:
  owner: principal
  body: text 1..200
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  online only
mutation add(body: text 1..200):
  return insert notes { owner: viewer, body }
query myNotes(c: cursor ?):
  return notes where .owner = viewer first 20 by .id after c
```

Use `serve` for staging, shared previews and `probe:serve`. It enforces the same
password identity and rules as production. `dev` always advertises
`development:true` in `/auth/config`, even with closed doors; probes should
refuse it. `guide deploy` covers release evolution; PROD2 owns starter adaptation.

## Password roles and operator provisioning

Save this complete source as `snapback/schema.q` in an app with snapback4 installed.
Signup creates an account without a role. Only the local filesystem operator can
bootstrap this roster. The insert rule enforces the boundary independently of
the mutation. SEC owns self-promotion diagnostics and invariant proofs.

## Stand up staging

Run `npx snapback4 check` to generate the API. Save this as `staging.mjs` next to
snapback/, then run `node staging.mjs`. Use `node staging.mjs --once` for CI setup
and shutdown. The deployment and private credentials file remain for inspection;
choose fresh names for each independent run. Add both to .gitignore. PORT_LO and
PORT_HI optionally choose a port range. The printed URL belongs to `serve`.

```js
import { openSync, writeFileSync, closeSync } from "node:fs";
import { deploy, startServe } from "snapback4/proof";
import { api } from "./snapback/generated/api.ts";

await deploy("staging", "snapback", { args: ["--staging"] });
const host = await startServe("staging", { seedUsers: {
  roles: { admin: 2, member: 2 }, provision: api.provision,
} });
try {
  // This file contains real passwords and bearer tokens. Never commit it.
  const fd = openSync("staging-users.json", "wx", 0o600);
  try { writeFileSync(fd, JSON.stringify(host.sessions)); } finally { closeSync(fd); }
  for (const accounts of Object.values(host.sessions)) {
    for (const account of accounts) {
      const response = await fetch(host.url + "/m/add", {
        method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json", authorization: `Bearer ${account.token}` },
        body: JSON.stringify({ args: { body: `Welcome ${account.role}` } }),
        signal: AbortSignal.timeout(10000),
      });
      if ((await response.json()).state !== "sent") throw Error("staging data write refused");
    }
  }
  console.log(JSON.stringify({ backendUrl: host.url, credentialsFile: "staging-users.json" }));
  if (!process.argv.includes("--once")) await new Promise(resolve => {
    process.once("SIGTERM", resolve); process.once("SIGINT", resolve);
  });
} finally { await host.stop(); }
```

`host.seedAccounts({roles, provision, args?})` performs additional batches; an
optional `args(account)` merges over `{principal, role}` (explicit keys win).
Use `service: "ops"` for a provisioning mutation authorized as `service:ops`.
Each call creates new random email/password pairs and returns role arrays of
`{role,email,password,principal,token,expiresAt}`. Ordinary sessions last seven
days; sign in again with `login` from snapback4/auth when needed. Setup is
sequential and bounded (default five minutes, timeoutMs overrides it, max 100
accounts). Identity 429 refusals wait for retryAfterMs (Retry-After on older
servers), at most four attempts per account within the remaining setup budget.
SDK guest/signup/login share this policy (timeoutMs defaults to three minutes;
signal cancels a request or wait). Ambiguous writes never retry. Partial failure can leave accounts/grants: inspect or discard this
disposable deployment before retrying. Returned credentials never enter helper
logs. Print them only to a private operator destination, as above.

## Production refuses bulk setup

`deploy --staging` can classify only a new store. Existing unmarked stores are
production, and refuse relabelling. Classification stays with the store across
hot deploys, rollbacks and restarts; copying production into a directory named
staging does not change it. `deploy --check --staging` does not mark a store.
There is no promotion flag: deploy real production into its own fresh directory
and provision only intended users. `seedAccounts` and startServe's seedUsers
refuse production or dev before minting a token or creating any account. A
normal production startServe still supports operatorToken for explicit grants.

### E_STAGING

E_STAGING refuses production relabelling or bulk account setup on a non-staging
host. Choose a separate fresh `deploy --staging` store; use the explicit
first-admin recipe below for intended production users. Source edits and a
directory named staging never change a production store's classification.


### Account setup refusals

E_STAGING_INPUT means the account plan is invalid. Provide 1..100 accounts,
named roles, a declared mutation handle and a positive timeoutMs before setup.

E_STAGING_TIMEOUT means the setup deadline expired. Partial accounts/grants
may exist; inspect or discard the disposable deployment before retrying.

E_STAGING_SIGNUP means password signup refused or returned an invalid session.
Inspect the underlying refusal; partial accounts/grants may exist, so inspect
or discard the disposable deployment before retrying.

E_STAGING_PROVISION means the operator mutation refused or returned an invalid
reply. Repair its arguments and operator-only rules; partial accounts/grants
may exist, so inspect or discard the disposable deployment before retrying.

### Identity request deadline

E_IDENTITY_TIMEOUT is the internal sign-in deadline cause (SDK E_OFFLINE).
Signup outcome may be unknown: inspect before retrying. Increase timeoutMs to
allow server retryAfterMs waits. seedAccounts maps it to E_STAGING_TIMEOUT
with the partial-account recovery guidance above.

## First admin on production

Deploy the same schema with `npx snapback4 deploy production` (omit --staging).
Save the following as `first-admin.mjs`. Set FIRST_ADMIN_EMAIL and
FIRST_ADMIN_PASSWORD privately, then run `node first-admin.mjs` as the store
owner while no other serve owns its port. This script starts and stops serve;
restart it normally afterwards. For an already running deployment use
`snapback4 token --service operator --days 1 --release production` locally and
the same authenticated mutation. Keep the service token out of the frontend.

```js
import { signup, login } from "snapback4/auth";
import { startServe } from "snapback4/proof";
const email = process.env.FIRST_ADMIN_EMAIL, password = process.env.FIRST_ADMIN_PASSWORD;
if (!email || !password) throw Error("Set FIRST_ADMIN_EMAIL and FIRST_ADMIN_PASSWORD privately");
const host = await startServe("production");
try {
  // Login first so rerunning this script preserves the existing account and grant.
  let account = await login(host.url, email, password);
  if (!account.ok) account = await signup(host.url, email, password);
  if (!account.ok) throw Error(account.why.message);
  const token = await host.operatorToken();
  const response = await fetch(host.url + "/m/provision", {
    method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json", authorization: `Bearer ${token}` },
    body: JSON.stringify({ args: { principal: account.session.principal, role: "admin" } }),
    signal: AbortSignal.timeout(10000),
  });
  if ((await response.json()).state !== "sent") throw Error("operator grant refused");
  console.log("First admin provisioned; sign in with the chosen email and password");
} finally { await host.stop(); }
```

Reruns authenticate the same email/password and upsert the same roster row.
A wrong password refuses without changing an existing grant. No first-user race or self-promotion mutation is needed. Subsequent grants use
the same operator process or an explicitly authorized admin policy. Invite codes
must be server-validated, single-use grants issued by an authorized operator;
an arbitrary caller-supplied code or role is not authority.

## Local fixture tools are explicit

Password-only dev closes /seed, /dev/* and persona headers by default.
`dev --unsafe-dev-doors` explicitly opens local fixture and clock tools and
prints a warning; it still requires loopback Host/Origin, and personas must be
declared with `use personas`. The local token-mint protocol keeps its existing
private store-owner proof. Serve never opens these development tools.
`/auth/config` reports `environment`, `development`, and `devDoors` separately.
`startDev()` persists by default, matching native dev; pass args:["--memory"]
for disposable storage. now() needs the explicit clock door on password-only
dev. A proof for real users uses the serve recipe above.

## Time and reused stores

`await host.advance("49h")` returns `{now, fired, held}`; `await host.now()` still
returns a timestamp. The CLI `clock advance 49h --release staging` returns the
same outcomes. Fired means enqueued, so inspect each run state for parked work.
Every held schedule gives a reason and next boundary. Saved boundaries survive
restart: each boundary fires once; missed periods coalesce unless eachBoundary
is explicit. A reused store can hold a due application row because that window
already ran. The helper and CLI print the saved-boundary hold and recovery on
stderr. Advance beyond the saved boundary's next window, keep --clock-offset
across serve restarts when continuing, or deploy a fresh staging directory for
an independent test. Never replay a lost advance reply. Time travel also ages
sessions and unplaced uploads: log in again and upload bytes after a large jump.
## Proof API (generated from source)

Every runtime and type export of `snapback4/proof` is listed below. Signatures, result fields and failure reasons are generated from TypeScript declarations and their @throws comments; qualification checks this table against the source. Playwright types such as Page and Locator come from playwright-core.

| Signature | Result / fields | Failure reasons |
|---|---|---|
| `bounded<T>(work: Promise<T>, ms: number, label: string): Promise<T>` | `Awaited<T>` | deadline expires; work rejection propagates. |
| `cleanup(...steps: Array<(() => unknown \| Promise<unknown>) \| undefined>): Promise<void>` | `void` | AggregateError after every cleanup was attempted; each step has a 10-second bound. |
| `cli(args: readonly string[], options?: ProofOptions): Promise<CliResult>` | `{ code: number \| null; signal: NodeJS.Signals \| null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; } }` | spawn error or command deadline; nonzero exit is returned in code, not thrown. |
| `command(binary: string, args: readonly string[], options?: ProofOptions): Promise<CliResult>` | `{ code: number \| null; signal: NodeJS.Signals \| null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; } }` | spawn error or command deadline; nonzero exit is returned in code, not thrown. |

| `fillRN(locator: Locator, text: string): Promise<void>` | `void` | locator ambiguity, disabled/closed control, or Playwright deadline. |
| `frontPage(page: Page): Promise<Page>` | `Page` | page closed or browser activation failed. |

| `sandboxUnavailable(error: unknown): boolean` | `false or true` | None; returns a boolean for recognized sandbox launch errors. |

Partial accounts/grants may exist; inspect or discard the staging store before retrying. |
| `selectByValue(locator: Locator, value: string): Promise<void>` | `void` | locator ambiguity, unavailable option or Playwright deadline. |

| `startServe(deployDir: string, options?: ServeOptions \| readonly string[]): Promise<ServeHost>` | `ServeHost` | E_PROOF_USAGE for missing deployment directory; child exit, readiness deadline or served identity mismatch. stop() rejects bounded cleanup failure. |
| `startStatic(dir: string, options?: ProofOptions): Promise<ProofHost>` | `{ url: string; pid: number; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; }; stop: () => Promise<void> }` | missing export/index or build script precondition; exhausted ports, readiness deadline or served identity mismatch. |

//! Compile examples and audit source diagnostic coverage.
use super::*;

pub(super) fn compile(source: &str) -> snapback4_lang::Bound {
    snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".to_owned(), source.to_owned())])
}

pub(super) fn clean(label: &str, source: &str) -> snapback4_lang::Bound {
    let bound = compile(source);
    let list = |items: &[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic]| items.iter().map(ToString::to_string).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n");
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{label} does not compile:\n{}", list(&bound.diagnostics));
    // DX5 makes reader-only gates and private named conflicts visible in
    // existing examples. They remain runnable; pin the source and repair of
    // each advisory rather than calling an intentional privacy gate unclean.
    for note in bound.notes.iter().filter(|n| matches!(n.code.as_str(), "N_LOOKUP_FILTERED" | "N_UNIQUE_HIDDEN")) {
        let line = source.lines().nth(note.site.line.saturating_sub(1) as usize).expect("located source advice");
        assert_eq!(note.guide, "rules");
        assert!(note.rewrite.contains("private rows") && !note.rewrite.is_empty(), "{note}");
        if note.code == "N_UNIQUE_HIDDEN" { assert!(line.contains("unique"), "{line}: {note}"); }
        else { assert!(line.contains('[') || line.trim_start().starts_with("receipts "), "{line}: {note}"); }
    }
    let notes = if source.contains(&guide::language()) { guide_context::without_grouped_shape_note(&bound, "groupedMessages") }
        else { bound.notes.iter().collect::<Vec<_>>() };
    let notes: Vec<_> = notes.into_iter().filter(|n| !matches!(n.code.as_str(), "N_LOOKUP_FILTERED" | "N_UNIQUE_HIDDEN")).collect();
    // The populated-store feed deliberately teaches its sparse-scan limit.
    // Pin the actual card block and its one note; other examples stay clean.
    if quarry(card("feeds").unwrap()).iter().any(|block| block == source && block.contains("query home(") && !block.contains("view following:")) {
        assert_eq!(notes.len(), 1, "{label}: {:?}", bound.notes);
        assert_eq!(notes[0].code, "N_SPARSE_FILTER");
        return bound;
    }
    if quarry(card("offline-interactions").unwrap()).iter().any(|block| block == source && block.contains("view ownLikes:")) {
        assert_eq!((notes.len(), notes.first().map(|n| n.code.as_str())), (1, Some("N_MAINTAIN_SYNCED")), "{label}: {:?}", bound.notes);
        return bound;
    }
    // These exact published ordinary-search examples teach both coverage notes.
    // Keep their codes, counts and query site pinned; no general note exemption.
    let search_examples = quarry(card("search").unwrap());
    let package_examples = quarry(include_str!("../../../../packages/snapback4/README.md"));
    if search_examples.iter().skip(1).chain(package_examples.iter())
        .any(|block| block == source && block.contains("query search(q: text <=100, c: cursor ?):")) {
        let notes = if source.contains("query searchGrouped(") { guide_context::without_grouped_shape_note(&bound, "searchGrouped") } else { notes };
        let mut codes = notes.iter().map(|note| note.code.as_str()).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        codes.sort_unstable();
        assert_eq!(codes, ["N_NEVER_COMPLETE", "N_SYNC_ORDER"], "{label}: {:?}", bound.notes);
        assert!(notes.iter().all(|note| note.message.starts_with("query search")), "{label}: {:?}", bound.notes);
        return bound;
    }
    // The timed-peer recipe intentionally preserves a hidden-target upsert's
    // runtime deadline equality. Pin its one documented uncertainty, not a
    // general immutable-note exemption.
    if quarry(card("rule-recipes").unwrap()).iter().any(|block| block == source
        && block.contains("mutation reportWords(") && block.contains("table peerCounts:")) {
        assert_eq!(notes.len(), 1, "{label}: {:?}", bound.notes);
        let note = &notes[0];
        assert_eq!(note.code, "N_IMMUTABLE_PATCH");
        assert!(note.message.contains("peerCounts.opensAt"), "{note:?}");
        assert!(note.message.contains("equals the stored value"), "{note:?}");
        assert!(card("rule-recipes").unwrap().contains("runtime equality"));
        return bound;
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#14 — caller-visible existence is the lesson, not global absence.
    // This exact ladder example intentionally contrasts a hidden rule probe
    // with caller-visible existence; its one advisory note is part of the lesson.
    if quarry(card("rules").unwrap()).iter().any(|block| block == source && block.contains("query canReadLadderGrant(")) {
        assert_eq!(notes.len(), 1, "{label}: {:?}", bound.notes);
        assert_eq!(notes[0].code, "N_UNREADABLE_PROBE");
        assert!(notes[0].message.contains("canReadLadderGrant"));
        assert!(card("rules").unwrap().contains("caller-visible existence is intended here"));
        return bound;
    }
    // The live recipe deliberately teaches its full-current acquisition note.
    if source.contains("table liveRosterFiles:") {
        assert_eq!(notes.len(),1,"{label}: {:?}",bound.notes);
        assert_eq!(notes[0].code,"N_NO_HORIZON");
        assert!(notes[0].rewrite.contains("online only") && !notes[0].rewrite.contains("last 500"));
        return bound;
    }
    let unexpected: Vec<_> = notes.into_iter().filter(|note| !matches!(note.code.as_str(), "N_LOOKUP_FILTERED" | "N_UNIQUE_HIDDEN") && !(source.contains("ephemeral ") && note.code == "N_EPHEMERAL_SERVER")).cloned().collect();
    assert!(unexpected.is_empty(), "{label} compiles with notes:\n{}", list(&unexpected));
    bound
}

/// The sources a scan of literals covers: the Rust crates and the client.
pub(super) fn sources() -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    let root = Path::new(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")).join("../..");
    let mut files = Vec::new();
    fn walk(dir: &Path, files: &mut Vec<PathBuf>) {
        for entry in std::fs::read_dir(dir).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("{}: {e}", dir.display())) {
            let path = entry.unwrap().path();
            if path.is_dir() {
                if path.file_name().is_some_and(|n| n == "node_modules" || n == "target" || n == "dist" || n == "tests" || n == "test") {
                    continue;
                }
                // Differential-test records quote the Lean model's own
                // refusals (`E_MODEL_TYPE`), which no product path emits.
                if path.ends_with("snapback4-difftest/cases") {
                    continue;
                }
                walk(&path, files);
            } else if path.file_name().is_some_and(|n| n.to_string_lossy().ends_with("_tests.rs") || n.to_string_lossy().ends_with(".test.mjs")) {
                // A test module inside `src/` invents refusal codes for fixtures;
                // this check is about the codes the product itself emits.
                continue;
            } else if path.extension().is_some_and(|e| e == "rs" || e == "ts" || e == "tsx" || e == "md") {
                files.push(path);
            }
        }
    }
    for dir in ["crates", "packages/snapback4/src", "packages/snapback4-expo/src"] {
        walk(&root.join(dir), &mut files);
    }
    files.push(root.join("packages/snapback4/README.md"));
    assert!(files.len() > 40, "{} source files", files.len());
    files

# Client and operations

[Back to the package guide](./README.md).

For TypeScript actions use the generated `server()` wrapper described in
[server handlers](./SERVER.md); it enforces the application's precise handler
signatures. A custom runtime host can use the lower-level registry entry below
with that generated manifest and already checked handlers. It does not infer
application-specific argument/result types itself:

```ts
import { startServer, type ServerHandlers } from "snapback4/server";
import { manifest } from "../snapback/generated/server";

function hostCheckedHandlers(handlers: ServerHandlers, signal: AbortSignal) {
  return startServer(manifest, handlers, { signal });
}
```

## Offline web starter

`npx snapback4 init --web` writes a one-screen Vite + React todos app on
`createApp` with `webDevice()`; `npx snapback4 init --web --template chat`
copies the maintained Vite chat reference, including its production app shell,
account-bound saved route and write IDs. Default `init` remains schema-only.
Run `npm install`, `npx snapback4 check`, then
`npx snapback4 dev` and `npm run dev` in separate terminals. Build with
`VITE_SNAPBACK_URL=https://your-backend npm run build`; serve `dist/` over HTTPS.
The chat's routes use `#room=<id>`.

The build ends with `snapback4 web-shell dist`. Navigations use the active
worker's cached HTML even online, so its HTML and lazy assets stay together
until a waiting build activates after the old tabs close. This command also works on
`expo export -p web` output: it precaches every file in a content-hashed cache,
registers `snapback4-sw.js` in every HTML file idempotently, and resolves exported
subpages (`/contact` and `/contact/` to `contact.html`, then `/about/` to
`about/index.html` when `about.html` is absent) online and offline before falling
back to the root `index.html`. Pass `--base /chat/` when serving a subpath;
use the same base in the bundler. For Vite run
`npx vite build --base=/chat/ && npx snapback4 web-shell dist --base /chat/`.
It waits for old tabs to close before switching builds. Development has no worker.
Keep all exported files nonpersonalized; this is a static export, not SSR.
Cross-origin requests and non-exported API/media requests pass through untouched.
The worker never caches network navigation responses. The starter explicitly uses
`localStorageSessionStore()` for page-restart continuity; any script on that
origin can read the token. Choose this persistence deliberately.

Held room navigation reads conversation and membership before rendering the
thread; unknown offline rooms remain uncertain. Write references are saved
before SDK dispatch and reopened with `usePendingWrite(op, id)`, without
resubmission. `markRead` demonstrates a nonpredicting queued write. Failed and
unknown receipts stay visible; a settled write does not imply a fresh read.
Sign-out hides the account, completes only after local credentials are removed,
and keeps its replica/outbox intact. A storage failure stays visible for retry.
For a keyed text/media send, use the intent-card recipe below; keep saved failure references scoped to this account and backend.
See the generated README for residency tables, horizon choices and the
production offline-reopen journey; fixture residency remains
`test --residency --fixture <directory> --as <principal>`.

For a browser proof, register the application's own app and generated API
(starters do this; the hook is active only under `?witness=web`):

```ts
import { registerWebWitness, witnessForm } from "snapback4/witness-web";
import { createApp } from "snapback4/app";
# Client: answers stay on screen

**Start here:** `useQueryData`/`useQueryResult` ([short screens](CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md#short-screens)); metadata: `useQuery`/`usePage`; `useFollowing` (`guide feeds`); lists: `ReadState`.

```tsx
import { matchRead, readPresentation, usePage } from "snapback4/react";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
export function Inbox() { const page = usePage(api.inboxCards, {}), status = readPresentation(page.read); return <>{matchRead(page.read, { loading: () => "Loading…", denied: why => why.message,
    absent: () => "No conversations", partial: () => "Partial results", present: rows => `${rows.length} conversations` })}
    {status.incomplete && "Results may be incomplete"}{page.items.map(row => <p key={row.id}>{row.title} ({row.memberCount})</p>)}<button disabled={!page.hasMore} onClick={page.loadMore}>More</button></>;
}
```

- `E_OFFLINE`: unavailable; reconnect.
- `E_QUERY_RETRY`: exhausted; retry.

## Reference

`Write`: pending custody, fresh sent result, replay without result, or failed. Clear on sentResult; undefined is not success.
```tsx
import { useState } from "react"; import { matchRead, sentResult, useAppReady, useMutation, useQuery } from "snapback4/react";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
export function Composer({conversationId}:{conversationId:string}) {
  const [draft,setDraft]=useState(""),[submitted,setSubmitted]=useState("");
  const ready=useAppReady(),send=useMutation(api.send),member=useQuery(api.myMembership,{conversationId});
  const blocked=matchRead(member,{denied:()=>true,absent:read=>read.fresh&&!read.revalidating,partial:()=>false,otherwise:()=>false});
  async function submit(){const text=draft.trim();if(!ready||blocked||!text||text.length>2000)return;setSubmitted(text);const write=await send.run({conversationId,body:text});if(sentResult(write)!==undefined)setDraft(current=>current.trim()===text?"":current);}
  return <><input value={draft} onChange={e=>setDraft(e.target.value)}/>
    <button disabled={!ready||send.inFlight||blocked} onClick={()=>void submit()}>Send</button>
    {send.last?.state==="failed"&&submitted&&<p role="alert">Not sent: {submitted} <button onClick={()=>setDraft(current=>current?current+" "+submitted:submitted)}>Edit</button> <button onClick={()=>setSubmitted("")}>Dismiss</button></p>}</>;
}
```
Membership: no horizon, the conversation's own group and messages' audience prove existence unless a pending prediction blocks absence. `loading` means acquisition or later withdrawal. `existence(member) === "absent"` blocks only when fresh and non-revalidating; denied blocks; loading/partial never disable. Never add `with own keys` to admin-written memberships.
Draft/receipt owners: [reference](CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md). `waitSeconds:0` returns custody; keep the original id, never auto-resend.

## An online role form across releases
Own createApp({url,writes:"online",sessionStore:localStorageSessionStore()}) outside render under AppProvider.
```tsx
import { useEffect, useState } from "react"; import { isPresent, matchReadList, sentResult, useAppReady, useMutation, useQuery, useRelease } from "snapback4/react"; import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
export function StaffWorkspace({team}: {team: string}) {
  const [draft, setDraft] = useState(""), [selected, setSelected] = useState("");
  const ready = useAppReady(), release = useRelease(), role = useQuery(api.myRole, {team});
  const [lastStaff, setLastStaff] = useState(false);
  const staff = api.myRole.available(release) && isPresent(role) && role.complete && role.fresh && !role.revalidating && role.data.role === "staff", uncertain = "loading" in role || ("data" in role && (role.existence === "partial" || !role.complete || !role.fresh || role.revalidating === true));
  useEffect(() => { if (!uncertain) setLastStaff(staff); }, [uncertain, staff]);
  const shell = uncertain ? lastStaff : staff;
  const available = api.addStaffNote.available(release) && api.staffNotes.available(release), add = useMutation(api.addStaffNote);
  async function submit() {const title = draft.trim(); if (!ready || !staff || !available || !title || !selected) return;
    const write = await add.run({team, title, category:selected}); if (sentResult(write) !== undefined) setDraft(current => current === draft ? "" : current);}
  return <section aria-label="Staff workspace"><p>{shell ? "Staff workspace" : "Staff access required"}</p>
    <label>Note<input value={draft} onChange={e => setDraft(e.target.value)} /></label>
    <label htmlFor="category">Category</label><select id="category" value={selected} onChange={e => setSelected(e.target.value)}><option value="">Choose</option><option value="general">General</option></select>
    <button disabled={!ready || !staff || !available || add.inFlight} onClick={() => void submit()}>Save note</button>
    {staff && available ? <StaffRows team={team} /> : <p>{"denied" in role || "denied" in release || ("generation" in release && !available) || (!uncertain && !staff) ? "Staff workspace not available" : "Waiting for staff capabilities"}</p>}
    {add.last?.state === "failed" && <p role="alert">{add.last.why.message}</p>}
  </section>;
}
function StaffRows({team}: {team:string}) {
  const rows = useQuery(api.staffNotes, {team}); return <>{matchReadList(rows, {denied: why => <p role="alert">{why.message}</p>, absent: () => <p>No notes</p>,
    present: notes => <ul>{notes.map(row => <li key={row.id}>{row.title} — {row.priority ?? "normal"}</li>)}</ul>,
    otherwise: () => <p>Updating notes…</p>})}</>;
}
```
Mount `<StaffWorkspace key={team} team={team}/>`: drafts survive loading/releases; resources/accounts/backends reset them. Sticky role labels the shell; fresh/complete/non-revalidating role and availability gate reads/Save. Rules decide permission. Optional since-release fields need fallbacks: `row.priority ?? "normal"`.

## A public guest page

Guests (`use identity`/`use identity guests`): public reads require browser session custody.
```tsx
import { createApp } from "snapback4/app";
import { localStorageSessionStore } from "snapback4/auth";
import { AppProvider, matchReadList, useAuth, useQuery } from "snapback4/react";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
const guestApp = createApp({url:"http://127.0.0.1:4400", readOnly:true, sessionStore:localStorageSessionStore()});
export function GuestPage() { return <AppProvider app={guestApp}><GuestGate /></AppProvider>; }
function GuestGate() {
  const auth = useAuth();
  if (auth.state === "restoring") return <p>Restoring…</p>;
  if (auth.state === "signed-out") return <><button onClick={() => void auth.guest()}>Continue as a guest</button><p role="alert">{auth.why?.message}</p></>;
  return <PublicNotes />;
}
function PublicNotes() {
  const notes = useQuery(api.publicNotes, {});
  return <>{matchReadList(notes, {denied: why => <p role="alert">{why.message}</p>, absent: () => <p>No public notes</p>,
    present: rows => <ul>{rows.map(row => <li key={row.id}>{row.title}</li>)}</ul>, otherwise: () => <p>Loading…</p>})}</>;
}
```

## React hooks and auth decisions

Render sign-in controls while signed-out, Restoring… otherwise. AppProvider resets on app/viewer changes. `useAppReady()` marks successful initial opening after app.ready; release switches/admission warnings preserve it. Restoration, sign-out and app/account replacement reset it; initial failure recovers on sign-in. Keep drafts above role/release gates. Readiness grants no permission/admission.
`writes:"online"` with device/readOnly yields signed-out `useAuth().why` and failed app.ready. Recreate corrected. Missing browser storage still throws `E_ADMISSION`.
`useAuth()` members: `state`, `viewer`, `why`, `signup(email, password)`, `login(email, password)`, `guest()`, `logout()`, `logoutEverywhere()`. `signOut()` belongs to the client. Expo session store: secureStoreSessionStore(SecureStore); password form: [reference](CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md#password-react-and-expo).

| Helper | Input/result | Handlers/arguments |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `matchRead(read, handlers)` | Read or page; handler result | `loading(read)`, `denied(why,read)`, `absent(read)`, `partial(data,read)`, `present(data,read)`. Bare `why: Refusal`; data first, metadata second. All five required, even partial on single rows; `otherwise(read)` replaces omitted loading/absent/present only. |
| `matchReadList(read, handlers)` | List read or page | Requires `denied(why,read)` and `otherwise(read)`; omitted partial uses otherwise. |
| `otherwiseAll(fallback, overrides?)` | Spread into matchRead handlers | `fallback(read)` includes denied/partial; named handlers win in any order. |
| `usePage(op, args)` | Query with `c: cursor ?`; omit c from args | `{read, items, hasMore, loadMore}`; match page.read/page, render items, call loadMore(). No handlers. |

//! Bounded, read-only evidence from the store's own commit clock.
use snapback4_core::{Engine, RuntimeError};
use std::collections::BTreeSet;

const SKIPPED_PREVIEW: usize = 32;
const TOLERANCE_MS: i64 = 5_000;
const RECENT_COMMITS: usize = 64;
const TABLE_BYTES: usize = 4096;
const TABLE_NAMES: usize = 8;
const REPORT_ERROR_BYTES: usize = 1024;
// Reserve one MiB for the outer clock/work envelope inside the host encoder.
// The reply retains `schedules` and its fired/held partition.
const REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET: usize = (crate::serve::JSON_RESPONSE_CEILING - (1 << 20)) / 2;

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12 — inspect a fixed recent commit window, never application row values.
pub fn ahead(engine: &Engine, now: i64) -> Result<Option<String>, RuntimeError> {
    // Reverse primary-key traversal: no full scan, sort, row payloads or retained
    // historical stamp set. octet_length(column) uses SQLite record metadata;
    // unlike a text length/cast/substr, it need not load oversized payloads.
    let mut lead = 0;
    let mut tables = BTreeSet::new();
    let mut omitted = false;
    engine.store().visit_recent_commit_clock(now.saturating_add(TOLERANCE_MS), TABLE_BYTES as i64, RECENT_COMMITS as i64, |record| {
        let at = record.at;
        if at <= now.saturating_add(TOLERANCE_MS) { return Ok(()); }
        lead = lead.max(at.saturating_sub(now));
        let length = record.length.unwrap();
        if length > TABLE_BYTES as i64 { omitted = true; return Ok(()); }
        let bytes = record.touched.unwrap();
        match serde_json::from_slice::<Vec<String>>(&bytes) {
            Ok(names) => for name in names {
                if tables.contains(&name) { continue; }
                if tables.len() < TABLE_NAMES && name.len() <= 128 { tables.insert(name); }
                else { omitted = true; }
            },
            Err(_) => omitted = true,
        }
        Ok(())
    })?;
    if lead == 0 { return Ok(None); }
    let mut names = tables.into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>();
    if omitted { names.push("additional tables omitted".into()); }
    if names.is_empty() { names.push("no table names recorded".into()); }
    Ok(Some(format!(
        "N_CLOCK_STORE_AHEAD: recent commit stamps lead the current clock by up to {lead}ms (latest {RECENT_COMMITS} commits; tables: {}); use a fresh store per offset test ({}), or restart with the same --clock-offset (or dev --resume-clock) to continue",
        names.join(", "), crate::dev_clock::isolated_command())))
}

/// UTC calendar text for Unix milliseconds: schedule boundaries are UTC.
pub fn utc(ms: i64) -> String {
    let (days, rem) = (ms.div_euclid(86_400_000), ms.rem_euclid(86_400_000));
    // Civil-from-days (proleptic Gregorian), exact for the I53 range.
    let z = days + 719_468;
    let era = z.div_euclid(146_097);
    let doe = z - era * 146_097;
    let yoe = (doe - doe / 1_460 + doe / 36_524 - doe / 146_096) / 365;
    let doy = doe - (365 * yoe + yoe / 4 - yoe / 100);
    let mp = (5 * doy + 2) / 153;
    let day = doy - (153 * mp + 2) / 5 + 1;
    let month = if mp < 10 { mp + 3 } else { mp - 9 };
    let year = yoe + era * 400 + i64::from(month <= 2);
    format!("{year:04}-{month:02}-{day:02}T{:02}:{:02}:{:02}Z", rem / 3_600_000, rem % 3_600_000 / 60_000, rem % 60_000 / 1_000)
}

/// A positive duration in the units `clock advance` accepts, rounded up to a second.
pub fn span(ms: i64) -> String {
    let seconds = (ms.max(0) + 999) / 1_000;
    let (d, h, m, s) = (seconds / 86_400, seconds % 86_400 / 3_600, seconds % 3_600 / 60, seconds % 60);
    let parts: Vec<String> = [(d, "d"), (h, "h"), (m, "m"), (s, "s")].into_iter().filter(|(n, _)| *n > 0).map(|(n, unit)| format!("{n}{unit}")).collect();
    if parts.is_empty() { "0s".into() } else { parts.join("") }
}

/// Each schedule's saved boundary, in declaration order.
pub fn saved_boundaries(engine: &Engine) -> Vec<Option<i64>> {
    engine.schema().schedules.iter()
        .map(|schedule| {
            let shape = engine.store().schedule_shape(&schedule.name).ok().flatten();
            if shape.as_ref().is_some_and(|shape| *shape != snapback4_core::value::digest(schedule)) { return None; }
            engine.store().schedule_boundary(&schedule.name).ok().flatten()
        })
        .collect()
}

/// Preflight is bounded before the clock or worker leases change.
// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12.1 — only explicit execution enumerates all crossed windows; coalesced reports keep a bounded preview.
pub fn crossed_boundaries(engine: &Engine, before: &[Option<i64>], now: i64, held: bool, each_boundary: bool) -> Result<Vec<(usize, i64)>, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    let mut boundaries = Vec::new();

        // JSON may escape every error byte; count that bound per schedule.

        if report_bytes > REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET { return Err(crate::dev_clock::input("schedule report exceeds its byte budget; reduce the declared schedules")); }
        if held { continue; }
        let Some(old) = before.get(index).copied().flatten() else { continue; };
        let end = schedule.boundary(now);

#!/usr/bin/env node
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#14 — resolve once, pin core and adapters to that same release.
import { upgradeWarnings } from './upgrade-warnings.js';
import { synopsis } from './cli-usage.js';
import { readFileSync, existsSync, writeFileSync, mkdirSync, readdirSync } from 'node:fs';
import { dirname, join } from 'node:path';
import { createRequire } from 'node:module';
import { spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';
import { previousVersion, installedVersion, acknowledgeVersion, compareVersions, installedHistory, runtimeChanges } from './release-changes.js';

let migrationWarnings = [];
async function run() {
  const args = process.argv.slice(2).flatMap(arg => arg.startsWith('--to=') ? ['--to', arg.slice(5)] : [arg]);
  if (args.length === 1 && args[0] === '--help') { console.log(`usage: ${synopsis('upgrade')}\nUpgrade snapback4 and every installed snapback4-expo adapter with npm, bun or pnpm; keep exact pins. Default: latest; refuses downgrades unless --to is explicit. Rebuild native apps after changing the adapter.`); return; }
  const json = args.includes('--json');
  const quiet = args.includes('--quiet');
  const input = args.filter(arg => arg !== '--json' && arg !== '--quiet');
  const explicit = input[0] === '--to';
  const target = explicit ? input[1] : input[0];
  if (input.length !== (explicit ? 2 : target ? 1 : 0) || (explicit && target === 'latest') || (target && !/^(latest|\d+\.\d+\.\d+(?:-[0-9A-Za-z.-]+)?)$/.test(target))) throw Error('E_INPUT: upgrade takes [--to <exact version>] [--json] [--quiet]');
  const log = text => { if (!quiet || text.startsWith('project ') || text.startsWith('Exact device')) (json ? process.stderr : process.stdout).write(text + '\n'); };
  let root = process.cwd();
  while (!existsSync(join(root, 'package.json'))) { const parent = dirname(root); if (parent === root) throw Error('E_UPGRADE: no project package.json'); root = parent; }
  const pkg = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(root, 'package.json'), 'utf8'));
  const previous = previousVersion(root);
  const installed = installedVersion(root);
  const schema = existsSync(join(root, 'snapback')) && readdirSync(join(root, 'snapback')).some(file => file.endsWith('.q'));
  // Quarry declarations are whole lines. Scan every authored module; a comment
  // mentioning identity never turns a persona-only project into an account app.
  const declarations = schema ? readdirSync(join(root, 'snapback')).filter(file => file.endsWith('.q')).map(file => readFileSync(join(root, 'snapback', file), 'utf8')).join('\n') : '';
  const personaOnly = /^use\s+personas\b/m.test(declarations) && !/^use\s+identity\b/m.test(declarations);

  // Resolve in a fresh Node process: pnpm can replace the package symlink,
  // while this helper's module/realpath cache still points at the old release.
  const checkScript = `
import { createRequire } from 'node:module';
import { dirname, join } from 'node:path';
import { spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';
try {
  const root = process.argv[1], require = createRequire(join(root, 'package.json'));
  const cli = join(dirname(dirname(require.resolve('snapback4/bin-path'))), 'bin/snapback4.js');
  const result = spawnSync(process.execPath, [cli, 'check', ...process.argv.slice(2)], { cwd: root, stdio: 'inherit' });
  process.exit(result.status ?? 1);
} catch { console.error('E_UPGRADE: cannot load installed snapback4 for check'); process.exit(1); }
`;
  const check = (baseline = false) => spawnSync(process.execPath, ['--input-type=module', '-e', checkScript, root, ...(baseline || json ? ['--json'] : [])], { cwd: root, env: { ...process.env, ...(baseline ? { SNAPBACK4_UPGRADE_BASELINE: '1' } : {}) }, encoding: 'utf8', stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'] });
  const declared = pkg.packageManager?.split('@')[0];
  const locks = [['bun', ['bun.lock', 'bun.lockb']], ['pnpm', ['pnpm-lock.yaml']], ['npm', ['package-lock.json', 'npm-shrinkwrap.json']]].filter(([, files]) => files.some(file => existsSync(join(root, file)))).map(([pm]) => pm);
  if (declared && !['npm', 'bun', 'pnpm'].includes(declared)) throw Error('E_UPGRADE: supported package managers are npm, bun and pnpm');
  if (!declared && locks.length > 1) throw Error('E_UPGRADE: multiple package-manager lockfiles; set packageManager in package.json');
  const pm = declared ?? locks[0] ?? 'npm';
  const dependencies = ['dependencies', 'devDependencies', 'optionalDependencies'];
  const targets = ['snapback4', 'snapback4-expo'].filter(name => name === 'snapback4' || dependencies.some(key => pkg[key]?.[name]) || existsSync(join(root, 'node_modules', name, 'package.json')));
  const invoke = (command, capture = false) => {
    const result = spawnSync(pm, command, { cwd: root, encoding: 'utf8', timeout: 300_000, stdio: capture || quiet ? ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'] : json ? ['ignore', 2, 2] : 'inherit' });
    if (result.error || result.status !== 0) {
      if (quiet && result.stdout) (json ? process.stderr : process.stdout).write(result.stdout);
      if (quiet && result.stderr) process.stderr.write(result.stderr);
      throw Error(`E_UPGRADE: ${pm} ${command[0]} failed; check registry/tool availability`);
    }
    return result.stdout;
  };
  let version = target ?? 'latest';
  if (version === 'latest') {
    const text = invoke(pm === 'bun' ? ['info', 'snapback4@latest', 'version', '--json'] : ['view', 'snapback4@latest', 'version', '--json'], true).trim();
    try { const data = JSON.parse(text); version = typeof data === 'string' ? data : data.version; }
    catch { throw Error('E_UPGRADE: package manager returned no exact core version'); }
  }
  if (typeof version !== 'string' || !/^\d+\.\d+\.\d+(?:-[0-9A-Za-z.-]+)?$/.test(version)) throw Error('E_UPGRADE: registry returned no exact core version');
  if (!explicit && installed && compareVersions(version, installed) < 0) throw Error(`E_UPGRADE: requested ${version} is older than installed ${installed}; refusing a downgrade. Use --to ${version} to request it explicitly.`);
  // Check the adapter release before any install changes package.json.
  for (const name of targets) invoke(pm === 'bun' ? ['info', `${name}@${version}`, 'version', '--json'] : ['view', `${name}@${version}`, 'version', '--json'], true);
  const pins = new Map(targets.map(name => [name, version]));
  if (targets.includes('snapback4-expo')) {
    const info = (name, field) => {
      const output = invoke(pm === 'bun' ? ['info', name, field, '--json'] : ['view', name, field, '--json'], true);
      try { return JSON.parse(output); } catch { throw Error(`E_UPGRADE: registry returned invalid ${field} for ${name}`); }
    };
    const peers = info(`snapback4-expo@${version}`, 'peerDependencies');
    const peer = peers?.expo;
    const floor = typeof peer === 'string' && /^(?:>=|~|\^)?(\d+\.\d+\.\d+)(?: |$)/.exec(peer)?.[1];
    if (!floor) throw Error('E_UPGRADE: adapter Expo peer has no supported exact floor');
    const current = dependencies.map(section => pkg[section]?.expo).find(Boolean);
    const installedExpo = current?.match(/^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$/)?.[0];
    // Preserve a newer compatible SDK patch; otherwise select the adapter floor.
    const expo = installedExpo && installedExpo.split('.')[0] === floor.split('.')[0] && compareVersions(installedExpo, floor) >= 0 ? installedExpo : floor;
    pins.set('expo', expo);
    const tarball = info(`expo@${expo}`, 'dist.tarball');
    const response = await fetch(tarball, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(30_000) });
    if (!response.ok) throw Error(`E_UPGRADE: could not read Expo ${expo} alignment manifest`);
    const unpack = spawnSync('tar', ['-xzOf', '-', 'package/bundledNativeModules.json'], { input: Buffer.from(await response.arrayBuffer()), encoding: 'utf8', timeout: 30_000 });
    let bundled;
    try { if (unpack.status !== 0) throw Error(); bundled = JSON.parse(unpack.stdout); }
    catch { throw Error(`E_UPGRADE: Expo ${expo} has no readable alignment manifest`); }
    const aligned = new Set(dependencies.flatMap(section => Object.keys(pkg[section] ?? {})));
    // Router's wildcard optional peers otherwise select an incompatible Worklets.
    if (aligned.has('expo-router')) for (const name of ['react-native-reanimated', 'react-native-worklets']) {
      if (!(name in bundled)) throw Error(`E_UPGRADE: Expo ${expo} has no Router alignment for ${name}`);
      aligned.add(name);
    }
    for (const name of Object.keys(bundled)) if (aligned.has(name)) {
      const range = bundled[name];
      const exact = typeof range === 'string' && /^[~^]?(\d+\.\d+\.\d+)$/.exec(range)?.[1];
      if (!exact) throw Error(`E_UPGRADE: Expo alignment for ${name} has no exact supported pin: ${range}`);
      pins.set(name, exact);
    }
    // The old Expo runtime may recommend an older core than the adapter needs.
    // Pin the adapter's Expo-module floor too, including newly required peers.
    for (const [name, range] of Object.entries(peers)) if (name.startsWith('expo-')) {
      const minimum = typeof range === 'string' && /^(?:>=|~|\^)?(\d+\.\d+\.\d+)(?: |$)/.exec(range)?.[1];
      if (!minimum) throw Error(`E_UPGRADE: adapter peer ${name} has no supported exact floor`);
      if (!pins.has(name) || compareVersions(pins.get(name), minimum) < 0) pins.set(name, minimum);
    }
    if (!quiet) log(`upgrade plan: snapback4/snapback4-expo → ${version}; expo ${current ?? '(absent)'} → ${expo}; align ${[...pins].filter(([name]) => !targets.includes(name)).map(([name, pin]) => `${name}@${pin}`).join(', ')}`);
  }
  const warnings = migrationWarnings = compareVersions(version, "0.4.33") >= 0 && (!installed || compareVersions(installed, version) < 0) ? upgradeWarnings(root, declarations) : [];
  for (const warning of warnings) process.stderr.write(`project ${pkg.name ?? root}: upgrade warning: ${warning}\n`);
  if (previous) acknowledgeVersion(root, previous);
  if (schema && installed === previous) {

for absent, hidden or expired rows. **`t[key] ! else CODE` refuses with CODE**
for the same three cases. This applies in queries and mutations and through
helpers. `exists t[key]` in a program is caller-visible; a rule's `exists`
is the separate rule-witness mechanism (§14.1). An id never grants authority.
There is no new optional-lookup operator or hidden-row error: the optional
form already exists. A later require cannot replace an earlier refusal.

Public refusal data must never distinguish hidden from missing, name a
runtime hiding clause only on a hit, disclose a key, or change a refusal's
meter according to existence. Static check diagnostics may quote authored
rules and source locations: they depend only on source, not stored rows.
Developer refusal traces remain local operator evidence. A blind write is
not a lookup and must not acquire a warning suggesting wider read access.

Extend program lookup advice beyond required same-table write targets.
**N_LOOKUP_FILTERED** identifies a mutation's optional point read of a
non-public side table (a table the operation does not write) when its key and
current binder flow facts do not prove read authority. Required direct point
reads keep existing N_LOOKUP_HIDDEN advice rather than duplicate this note;
the assertion already makes refusal explicit. It names
the operation, source point, target read rule and its declaration, and the
consequence (null before a later field/default or require).
Use the binder's actual point/key and current flow proofs, including helpers;
read-through side tables are included and name their authored inherited rule.
Ephemeral and maintained-value reads retain their specialized existing advice.

    pub fn fire_schedule_at(&mut self, now: i64, index: usize) -> Result<Vec<String>, RuntimeError> {
        self.fire_schedule_range(now, index..index.saturating_add(1), None)
    }

    /// Admit one historical period on the current clock. In particular an
    /// action's deadline begins at admission, not at the historical boundary.
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12.1 — period identity and admission time are distinct during explicit catch-up.
    pub fn fire_schedule_boundary_at(&mut self, now: i64, index: usize, boundary: i64) -> Result<Vec<String>, RuntimeError> {
        let schedule = self.schema.schedules.get(index).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input("unknown schedule index".into()))?;
        if schedule.boundary(boundary) != boundary || boundary > schedule.boundary(now) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Input("schedule window must end at an elapsed boundary".into()));
        }
        self.fire_schedule_range(now, index..index.saturating_add(1), Some(boundary))
    }

    fn fire_schedule_range(&mut self, now: i64, range: std::ops::Range<usize>, selected: Option<i64>) -> Result<Vec<String>, RuntimeError> {
        let mut fired = Vec::new();
        for schedule in self.schema.schedules.get(range).unwrap_or(&[]).to_vec() {
            let boundary = selected.unwrap_or_else(|| schedule.boundary(now));
            let shape = digest(&schedule);
            let program = self.programs.get(&schedule.target).filter(|p| matches!(p.kind, ProgramKind::Job | ProgramKind::Action))
                .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("schedule {} names missing job or action {}; restore its target before firing it", schedule.name, schedule.target)))?.clone();
            if program.kind == ProgramKind::Action && !schedule.service.as_deref().is_some_and(crate::identity::is_service) {
                return Err(RuntimeError::Schema("a scheduled action requires its declared service seat".into()));
            }
            if program.kind == ProgramKind::Job && schedule.service.is_some() {
                return Err(RuntimeError::Schema("a scheduled job runs as the system, not a service seat".into()));
            }
            let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
            let previous_shape: Option<String> = tx.schedule_shape(&schedule.name)?;
            if previous_shape.as_ref().is_some_and(|previous| previous != &shape) {
                tx.set_schedule_boundary(&schedule.name, boundary)?;
                tx.set_schedule_shape(&schedule.name, &shape)?;
                tx.commit()?;
                continue;
            }
            tx.set_schedule_shape(&schedule.name, &shape)?;
            let last: Option<i64> = tx.schedule_boundary(&schedule.name)?;
            if last.is_some_and(|last| last >= boundary) {
                tx.commit()?;
                continue;
            }
            let Some(_) = last else {
                tx.set_schedule_boundary(&schedule.name, boundary)?;
                tx.commit()?;
                continue;
            };
            let mut args = BTreeMap::new();
            if program.args.contains_key("periodStart") {
                args.insert("periodStart".to_owned(), Value::Int(schedule.previous(boundary)));
            }
            if program.args.contains_key("periodEnd") {
                args.insert("periodEnd".to_owned(), Value::Int(boundary));
            }
            let id = crate::ids::mint(now, &mut self.minter)?;
            if program.kind == ProgramKind::Action {
                let principal = schedule.service.as_deref().expect("checked service seat");
                validate_inputs(&self.schema, &program, &mut args)?;
                super::actions::authorize(&program, principal)?;
                let raw = serde_json::Value::Object(crate::wire::map_to_json(&args));
                let context = Context::new(principal, args).at(now);
                // @ref LLP 3000.009#5.2 — the boundary and service-seat admission are one commit.
                super::actions::insert_request(&tx, &self.schema, &program, &id, &context, &raw, true, now)?;
            } else {
                #[cfg(feature = "native")]
                let program_hash = job_admission_hash(&program, self.native.as_deref().map(super::native::Module::digest));
                #[cfg(not(feature = "native"))]
                let program_hash = job_admission_hash(&program, None);
                tx.insert_job(&id, &schedule.target, &args, &program_hash, now)?;
            }
            tx.set_schedule_boundary(&schedule.name, boundary)?;
            tx.commit()?;
            fired.push(schedule.name.clone());
        }
        Ok(fired)
    }
}

/// the state it returned last time, inside one transaction under the job
/// ceilings; its rows and events commit with the state, a refusal parks
/// it, a trap parks it with the reason. Over the engine's fields, not the
/// engine, because the transaction borrows its store.

    // This marker suppresses advice only; it cannot authorize a runtime read
    // or enter the binder's authority/presence proof domains.
    pub(super) fn lookup_viewer_key(&self, value: &A) -> bool {
        if matches!(value, A::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer" || self.binding(name)
            .is_some_and(|binding| binding.fields.contains_key(&vec![VIEWER_KEY.into()]))) { return true; }
        let equal = A::Binary { op: "=".into(), left: Box::new(value.clone()), right: Box::new(A::Name { name: "viewer".into(), site: value.site().clone() }), site: value.site().clone() };
        self.point_guard_safe(value) && self.authorization_proved(&equal, true)
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#10-wave-20-loop-repairs-dx5 — asserted and same-table reads still need reader authority; blind writes do not pass here.
    pub(super) fn optional_lookup_note(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, point: &Read) {
        if self.caller_kind != ProgramKind::Mutation || self.point_present(read) || self.lookup_point_present(read)
            || self.row_independent_read_proved(&read.table) { return; }
        let table = &self.schema.tables[&read.table];
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() || self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table).is_some() { return; }
        let index = &table.indexes[&point.index];
        let pinned = index.components.iter().zip(&point.key).filter_map(|(part, value)| part.column().map(|name| (name, value))).collect();
        if snapback4_core::engine::rules::prefix_visibility(&read.table, table, &table.rules.read, &pinned).decided_true { return; }
        if let Some(rule) = self.catalog.tables[&read.table].decl.rules.iter().find(|rule| rule.effect == ast::Effect::Read) {
            let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
            let keys: BTreeMap<_, _> = read.keys.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(at, key)|
                Some((if named { key.column.clone()? } else { index.components.get(at)?.column()?.clone() },
                    if self.lookup_viewer_key(&key.value) { A::Name { name: "viewer".into(), site: key.site.clone() } } else { key.value.clone() }))).collect();
            let mut condition = rule.value.clone();
            // Rule constants and target fields occupy their own namespaces.
            // Freeze/seal them before injecting lexical caller key expressions.
            maintain_types::normalize_rule_constants(&mut condition);
            super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |value| {
                if let A::Member { base, .. } = value && let A::Focal { site } = base.as_ref() {
                    **base = A::Name { name: TARGET_ROW.into(), site: site.clone() };
                }
            });
            super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |value| {
                if let A::Member { base, name, .. } = value && matches!(base.as_ref(), A::Name { name, .. } if name == TARGET_ROW)
                    && let Some(key) = keys.get(name) { *value = key.clone(); }
            });
            if self.lookup_rule_proved(&condition) { return; }
        }
        let rule = table.through.as_ref().map_or_else(|| format!("read <- {}", table.rules.read.quarry()), |through| format!("read through .{}", through.reference));
        let location = self.catalog.tables[&read.table].decl.rules.iter().find(|rule| rule.effect == ast::Effect::Read)
            .map(|rule| rule.site.to_string()).unwrap_or_else(|| self.catalog.tables[&read.table].decl.site.to_string());
        if self.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_LOOKUP_FILTERED" && note.site == read.site) { return; }
        self.notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "N_LOOKUP_FILTERED",
            format!("mutation {}: lookup of {} uses `{rule}` ({location}); its key and preceding guards do not prove read authority. A hidden, expired or missing row returns null; `! else CODE` refuses with that authored code for all three. A later field/default or require may report a different business condition", self.name, read.table),
            "review whether these callers should read this data; use a deliberately readable projection containing only the needed fields, or keep null as intentional absence. An id and a later write rule do not grant read authority; do not widen private rows just to read one field").guide("rules"));
    }

## 2. A short common read

Wave-20 list presentation addition: LLP 3000.000.000.001 §10 defines the
packaged ReadState component; the query hooks below retain their contracts.

Add `useQueryData(op, args, options?)` in `snapback4/react`, shared by React
and Expo. It delegates directly to `useQuery` and returns `R | undefined`:
loading gives undefined; present, absent and partial give their exact data
(including null, empty lists, false and zero). A denied read throws
`QueryRefusal extends Error`, carrying the original typed `why: Refusal`.
An error boundary may render it; callers choosing inline refusal UI use
`useQueryResult`, which returns `{data: R | undefined, why: Refusal | null,
read: Read<R>}` without throwing. These hooks do not substitute an empty list for loading/refusal. They add
no cache, previous-argument retention or observation ownership. `read` is the same snapshot as `useQuery`.

A data-only read does not promise completeness or freshness. It is for
screens that show the authorized rows that arrived. Pagination, absence proofs,
and actions depending on fresh or complete results continue to use `useQuery`
or `usePage` and their metadata. The precise API remains unchanged. Skipping
uses the existing typed arguments and `{skip: true}`. Required/mistyped
arguments still fail typechecking; result types come from generated operations.
`useMutation(op).run` is already a typed callable and remains unchanged.

Add `<SignedIn fallback={...} restoring={...}>...</SignedIn>` inside
AppProvider (bare SnapbackProvider is insufficient), using the same
`useAuth()` snapshot as AppProvider. Signed-in children mount only in that
state; restoring defaults to null, signed-out uses fallback. The existing
AppProvider account boundary resets account-owned state. Children must be JSX
components (React elements), not eagerly invoked component functions.
No bundled login UI or new session runtime is added to the React entry.

## 3. Completeness notes
### 7.2 Wave 3 (0.4.18): init first, schema second

Wave-20 custody amendment: LLP 3000.000.000.001 §10 keeps a runnable frontend
proof scaffold across adapt and moves Expo Metro/native smoke support into
the adapter package; edited application files remain owned by the app.

Wave 3 of the app farm (100 agents on 0.4.18) showed that §7.1's fixes fire
only when the schema exists *before* `init`. The README and the brief lead
agents to `init --web/--expo` first; they then replace `snapback/schema.q` and
are left with the todo starter's `fixtures/todos.jsonl` and cases (`test` runs
nothing), a guest sign-in screen over a persona-only schema (`auth.guest()`
refuses `E_AUTH`), or, rerunning init to get the picker, `E_INIT_CONFLICT`
(21 lanes, 41 minutes). The decisions, which make the outcome independent of
the order:

- **Leftovers are judged by the starter's bytes.** A starter file is a
  leftover only while it is byte-identical to what init wrote (the screen
  and client compared after normalizing the backend port and the persona
  list, the two values init varies) *and* it no longer fits the schema: the
  fixtures and cases when no `todos` table with `list`/`add`/`toggle`/`remove`
  exists; the screen and client when the schema's sign-in wants the other
  screen (guest for `use identity` with guests, the persona picker for
  `use personas` without `use identity`, or a picker for a different cast).
  A file the project changed is never reported as a leftover and never
  touched. No manifest is written: the starter text embedded in the binary is
  the reference, so a project that has never run 0.4.19's init still qualifies.
- **`check` names them, `init --adapt` fits them.** A successful `check`
